| Real coarse emulation (the DevTools protocol) | 1778 of 2120 controls reach 44 × 44 px and the 342 that do not are the stated exceptions, 318 admin-only (48 of the 48 table row and header checkboxes, compact; 4 of the 64 admin sidebar rows, comfortable; 62 of the 64 admin sidebar rows, compact; 92 of the 92 table rows, compact; 56 of the 56 admin table name-link cells, comfortable; 56 of the 56 admin table name-link cells, compact) and 24 links in running prose (WCAG 2.5.8's inline exception); every other student-route control reaches 44, the compact tool chrome (tabs, Find, Next match, Send, the page and search fields), the S-02 drawer's file rows and the comment pins included | `.design-foundations/tokens/coarse-audit.mjs` → `generated/coarse-audit.json` |
| Real forced-colours emulation | 240 selected, pressed, current or checked controls keep a visible cue (box-shadow markers left on chrome: 0; 124 redrawn as a ≥ 2 px outline, the rest by a glyph, a CanvasText dot or dash, weight 600 or the thumb's border; the 4 marks on the paper are content opted out with forced-color-adjust: none and keep their fixed G5 colours) | `.design-foundations/tokens/forced-audit.mjs` → `generated/forced-audit.json` |
| Detector (informational; the gate is the independent review) | 232 findings: `nested-cards` 230, every one a control inside a sheet (outlined secondary and destructive buttons, the composer's buttons, the neutral "In context" chip, the page and search fields, the drawer's filter chips): the case the lock's "Controls are not cards" rule whitelists; `em-dash-overuse` 1 (the sheet's body holds no em-dash character; the rule counts the `--` of the token names quoted in the notes); `numbered-section-markers` 1 (the tables' dates and times) | `detect.mjs` |

### Hand-offs

- **Phase 4 (Companion):** register `companion.slot.*` (launcher, composer peek, sign-in, empty-state, not-found) and `companion.backing`; this section fixes the surround: the 32 px avatar slot with a 12 px gap in the tutor row, the empty frame's 24 px below the slot, the launcher's 56 px disc; the companion's error pose fills the error frame; the one-mover rule extends to the toolbar (nothing else animates while an answer streams).
- **Phase 5 (Words):** every string quoted in the component tables and on the sheet is a placeholder, including the disabled reasons ("Enter at least one email first", "You're on the first page", …), error messages, the Stop label, the Display menu's labels ("Display settings", "Theme", "Kiwi animation"), the in-context chip and the email copy; the templates' colours are fixed here.
- **Phase 6 (Data surfaces):** apply the KPI tile (label, value, icon, trend or "no baseline") and the table rules (right-aligned tabular numerals in `--text`, units, truncation, the column minimums) to the analytics and admin tables; register `chart.*` with the five series at ≥ 3:1 on `--surface` in both modes, hue-separated and colour-blind-checked, replacing the interim `-11` stand-ins; the in-progress alert replaces the warning-toned "Analysing…" note.
- **Phase 7 (Mocks):** mocks paste `generated/kiwi-ui.tokens.css` and set `data-theme` and `data-density` on their roots; the phone-width renders (390 × 844) match `(pointer: coarse)` so the 44 px hit areas apply; no hard-coded hex (DW-7.2).
- **Implementation plan:**
  - port the emitter and checks (the four code-theme files replace `clouds`/`monokai` and `tomorrow`/`prism`; `Theme.test.ts` expects the interim `palette.chart` stand-ins until Phase 6);
  - migrate the ~350 literals, the PDF viewer's lilac greys and blue among them, and the MUI grey and `common` accesses (`§ Hand-off: MUI grey and common` below); retire `material-design-2014.json`;
  - render every disabled control with `aria-disabled` and its toggletip (no wrapper);
  - re-point `index.html`'s theme-color metas (one per scheme) and the manifest's colours to the surface and canvas tokens;
  - add the checkbox component's states and the Ace search-box rules (`ext-searchbox`, if the IDE loads it);
  - add the Stop control, the neutral in-progress indicator, the disabled reasons with their `aria-describedby` wiring, the PDF toolbar's stated boundaries (Previous on page 1), the DataGrid footer rules, the hit-area pseudo-elements and the empty-state frame;
  - apply the email mapping to both copies;
  - the sign-in aside drops its photo dependency (TD-19): the aside is the ruled reading column on `--surface` with the wordmark in `--accent-solid` (12.7:1 light / 8.3:1 dark), so the gradient-over-missing-photo failure cannot recur; with the carousel go `public/assets/images/bg-hero-1.svg`, `bg-hero-2.svg` and `bg-hero-3.svg`.

Followed to the letter today these canvases keep MUI's `#fafafa` / `#f5f5f5` in light and stay near-white in dark |

## Companion

**Phase 4 (2026-10-17), appended under the lock; fix round 1 and the round-2 review's follow-up applied the same day** (the user's decisions on the rendered sheet: `hop` about 1 s, `speaking` keeps the shallow dip, the like reaction is `wave`; the review's findings F1 to F10 and N5; then the round-2 Minors A-1 to A-13 except A-7, which waits for the user). The tutor companion as an honest, calm reflection of system state: nine chat states, the one-mover rule, the still poses, the motion controls, the placements, the backing, the asset budget and the ethics pass. It changes no locked value and no Phase 3 wording; it registers `companion.slot.*` and `companion.backing` in the DTCG source's reserved namespace, and the emitter and checker outputs follow (`§ Registration`). Plan: `.design-foundations/plans/2026-10-16-kiwi-ui-stage-1.md`, Phase 4 (DW-4.1 to DW-4.7); brief G9, G9b ("分时刻、读字时安静": react at moments, stay quiet while the student reads), G11, G14. Doctrine applied: motion (a clip ships only if it communicates a state change; whole drawings are swapped, nothing is tweened; reduced motion honoured at the source), ai-native (calibrate; every state that conveys information has a text twin; the canon gap is marked), behavioral (Norman's levels; Fogg's prompt timing; peak-end), deceptive-patterns (the nine Brignull categories; symmetric controls; no streak, nag or guilt), usability (Nielsen #1, #3, #4, #9; Fitts; recognition over recall). `md` below is the MUI `md` breakpoint, a 900 px viewport, as JOURNEY uses it.

**Amended 2026-10-18 (the user's decisions; plan Decision Log, the two newest companion rows).** The tutor avatar keeps one small, full-body framing. Every avatar state draws full-body drawings only, in one framing shared by `full-walk`, `full-hop-flight` and `full-wave-bow`, so the figure never changes size or place between states, and no `portrait-*` drawing remains in the avatar. Kiwi runs from the send until the answer is complete, as one loop through the wait, the tool steps and the streaming text; it hops and flies once at completion and lands on the standing still (`full-hop-flight` cell 23), which is also its idle still; every other state stands on that still. The one relaxation of the lock this needs is recorded in `§ Lock amendments`. Unchanged: one mover, the motion controls in their precedence, the composer peek, the launcher and the text twins, word for word. The page placements outside the chat panel (sign-in and register, the empty states and the page-level error frame, not found, S-08's card, the class-card fallback) keep their current stills on purpose: they are not the chat avatar sequence, never animate and never sit beside the avatar (`§ Placements`).

**Canon gap.** ai-native design has no settled canon. Claims marked `[principle-derived]` rest on practitioner sources (Dibia's agent-UX principles; Wilson, *Age of Invisible Machines*, 2022; Smashing, "Designing for AI Beyond Conversational", 2024) or on the designer's own inference, as stated; weight them accordingly. Everything else cites established doctrine (Nielsen 1994, Norman 1988/2013, Kahneman, Brignull 2023, WCAG 2.2).

**Source and assets.** The drawings are `NYUSH-ML/kiwi-ui` branch `kiwi-icon`, commit `fbb4db85f90aeda3a1250c8e9c8aa2d7473e2e11` ("feat: Add Kiwi icon and drawn-frame motion library"): eight registered atlases of 24 drawings each (6 × 4 cells of 256 px, a 24 Hz timeline clock with explicit exposure holds, no crossfade or pose warping), played by a frame player that honours reduced motion, hidden tabs, offscreen elements and interrupted reversal. The library is **ported**, not merged (G9); its chat mapping (`assistantState()`: thinking / speaking / error / idle) and its launcher peek are replaced by the table below, and its `KiwiLoader` is not used (a walk across a track toward an end that means nothing is a manufactured goal-gradient). Exported here, with a provenance note: `.design-foundations/mocks/assets/mascot/` (`still-stand.png`, the avatar's standing still, and the page placements' `still-idle.png`, `still-error.png` and `still-walk.png`, pixel-exact, with the inspection stills of `wave`, `peek` and `retract`; `stills/` the 2× WebPs of the sizes the placements load; `atlas/` resampled playback atlases; `launcher-glyph.svg`; `provenance.md`; `export-mascot.py`, which regenerates all of it from `--source <checkout>` and computes the avatar's shared framing). Evidence: `.design-foundations/mocks/companion-sheet.html` and `.design-foundations/mocks/screenshots/companion-*` (`§ Evidence`).

### Principles

1. **React at moments; still once the answer is complete (G9b, `§ Direction`; amended 2026-10-18).** Motion belongs to the moments the user named (typing, generating, arriving, liking, failing) and to the two system conditions (error, offline). The newest avatar runs from the send until the answer is complete, through the wait, the tool steps and the streaming text alike, as one loop: the user relaxed the lock's stillness while text streams for this one figure (`§ Lock amendments`). Once the turn ends (completed, stopped, failed or offline) the avatar stands, and it is still while the student reads the finished answer: `idle` is a still, not a blink loop, the lock's "still while text is on screen" applied to G9's "an occasional blink at most" (zero is within "at most"). `[principle-derived: designer's inference from G9b, the lock and the user's decision of 2026-10-18]`
2. **A clip ships only if it communicates a state change** (motion doctrine), with the two visceral exceptions the brief names (G9): the composer peek, which signals presence while the student composes and nothing else, and the like reaction, which acknowledges the student's own act. One loop is allowed, the run, because the state it shows is ongoing and the text alone does not show it: Kiwi is still answering, from the send until the answer is complete, including the pauses between steps and tokens; every other clip is one-shot and ends on the standing still. Not used: the library's `heart` (drawn as a bow; the user chose `wave` for the like), `bow`, `sleep`, `notification`, `curious`, `thinking`, `listening`, `fly`, `success`, `pop`, and since 2026-10-18 every `portrait-*` clip: none has an honest trigger in chat, and the avatar draws full-body drawings only.
3. **One mover.** Only the newest tutor message's avatar animates; older avatars hold their still. The composer peek is lower than the avatar: it shows only while the newest avatar is still, it is **cut, with no retract, the moment a send starts** (`walk` has priority), and it is removed, not stilled, when motion is off; reversal (`retract` from the drawing on screen) is for blur and for the typing flag's 1.5 s expiry only. The lock's chrome rule holds: while an answer streams nothing else in the panel animates. A like on an older message fills that thumb and plays no clip. In experiment compare mode only the avatar of the most recently started answer animates (ties: column A); the other column's avatar holds its still and its text twins carry its state, so the rule holds without exception.
4. **Never the only signal** (G9; WCAG 1.4.1). Every state that conveys information has a text twin (the table's last column; `peek`, `retract` and `idle` convey nothing, so their twin is "none"); the mascot is `aria-hidden="true"` everywhere and carries no `role`, `title` or label. Progress is stated in words, never implied by the drawing.
5. **Calibrated.** The companion never signals more than the system knows `[principle-derived: Dibia, Smashing]`: `hop` fires once per real completion and never per token; the run is in place (no progress bar, no journey) and stops at the next tick after the turn ends, completed, stopped, failed or offline, so it never outlasts the work; `offline` means "cannot answer now", not "you did something wrong"; `error` is apologetic, not alarming, and sits beside Retry (Nielsen #9): the error row says so, and the avatar only stops running.
6. **Transitions are cuts, and the player steps sprites.** Playback is a discrete swap of whole registered drawings at the 24 Hz clock (`background-position` moved by whole cells), nothing tweened: declared here as the one exception to the lock's rule that only `opacity` and `transform` move (`§ Motion`), since no property is animated between values. A state change is a hard cut, which is also the most honest "something changed" signal `[principle-derived: designer's inference from the library's no-interpolation rule and motion doctrine]`. Since 2026-10-18 every avatar cut stays inside one framing and between full-body drawings, so the figure keeps its size and place across it (`§ Registration`, the framing paragraph), and two moments need no cut at all: the first token, where the run carries on, and the end of the hop, which lands on the standing still. The one reversal the library implements: `peek` ↔ `retract` reverse from the drawing on screen, on blur or on the typing expiry (never on send). The run starts on its own and lasts as long as the answer, often longer than 5 s, so WCAG 2.2.2 (Pause, Stop, Hide) applies: the Display menu's Kiwi animation switch and the OS reduced-motion setting are the pause, and the deployment flag the deployment's.
7. **The artwork keeps its colours (G11).** The artwork's four colours are the pinned hue family, allowlisted in `§ Raw-colour guard`, not exact values once anti-aliased and resampled: `check-tokens.mjs` § 20 therefore also samples the lightest forest pixel of every shipped asset (`§ Backing and contrast`). No token recolours a drawing, and the backing is a UI token, not the mascot's cream. The launcher's glyph is an icon in `currentColor` (`§ Icon policy`), one path with no mask and no colour literal, not mascot art.

### Registration

Registered in `.design-foundations/tokens/kiwi-ui.tokens.json` under the reserved `companion` group (never edited into the three tiers); each token names its CSS property explicitly, so the resolver's naming rule is untouched; the emitter writes them into `generated/kiwi-ui.tokens.css` (`:root`, and `--companion-backing` again in both colour blocks). Dimensions are literals like `semantic.size`'s fixed sizes; the avatar references Phase 3's `semantic.size.avatar` (G9).

| Token | CSS | Light | Dark | Use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `companion.slot.avatar` | `--companion-slot-avatar` | `{semantic.size.avatar}` = 32 px | same | the tutor avatar on every tutor row (G9); the one animated placement in the message region |
| `companion.slot.peek` | `--companion-slot-peek` | 48 px | same | the composer peek, anchored to the composer field's top-right corner: its right edge on the field's right edge and its bottom edge on the field's top edge, inside the 48 px lane formed by the message region's 24 px bottom padding and the composer's 24 px top padding (raised from 12 px for the lane); shown only at scroll end, once per focus session (`§ The nine states`); framed with the drawing's wall (its inked columns end at x = 222 of 256) on the slot's right edge (frame left 13.28 %, width 100 %), not with the union-circle framing, so the head emerges from the edge (review A-3, re-checked at 2×) |
| `companion.slot.launcher` | `--companion-slot-launcher` | 28 px | same | the launcher's glyph box: the companion's portrait silhouette with a filled speech bubble (`launcher-glyph.svg`, `currentColor`) in `--accent-on-solid` on the locked 56 px `--accent-solid` disc; 28 and not the Phase 3 stand-in's 24 (`--launcher-icon-size`), decided on the sheet's 1× recognition check (review F6): the 24 px silhouette is a ghost (the eye cut-outs vanish), at 28 the beak reads but the eyes still do not, and the filled bubble restores the chat signifier (Nielsen #4; recognition over recall) while the head keeps "Kiwi"; the dotted bubbles fell below one pixel. The 14 px margin keeps the disc and its 44 px target unchanged. The glyph is one `currentColor` path with no mask and no colour literal: the body, bill and wing wound one way, the two eyes wound the other, so the nonzero rule leaves the eyes unfilled (review A-13; evenodd would also cut holes where the bill and wing overlap the body). Chrome: never animated, no backing |
| `companion.slot.signin` | `--companion-slot-signin` | 96 px | same | S-05, S-06: the idle still above the display line on the ruled column, from `md` |
| `companion.slot.empty` | `--companion-slot-empty` | 96 px | same | page-level empty states (S-01, A-01, A-19) and the page-level error frame, from `md` |
| `companion.slot.not-found` | `--companion-slot-not-found` | 96 px | same | S-10: the walk still (the standing bird), from `md` |
| `companion.slot.inline` | `--companion-slot-inline` | 64 px | same | the chat panel's empty conversation, the class-card fallback (S-01, A-01, A-02, A-13), S-08's centred card, and every 96 px slot below `md` (390 px) |
| `companion.backing` | `--companion-backing` | `{semantic.color.fill-none}` (transparent) | `#faf9f3`, frozen to light `global.color.paper.2` | the disc behind the drawn mascot, `--radius-round` at the slot size (the lock's dark-mode backing circle); under the avatar, the peek and every still; never under the launcher glyph |

**The dark backing is a frozen literal.** A light value cannot be referenced from the dark context in this resolver, so the dark value is a literal held to its source exactly as the on-paper marks are: `$extensions["org.kiwi"].frozen = { source: "{global.color.paper.2}", context: "light", at: "dark" }`. `check-tokens.mjs` gained the two checks that follow from the registration and nothing else: § 5 admits a `companion.*` colour literal only when it is frozen to a named light source, and § 15 reads a frozen literal held in a mode block (`frozen.at`); § 20 asserts the backing contrast (`§ Backing and contrast`). The MUI theme preview and the public CSS are unchanged (the companion is not a public token in Stage 1).

**Framing.** The tutor avatar draws every state in **one shared framing** (amended 2026-10-18): the library's own circle-fit rule (`framing.ts`: every alpha-positive pixel corner, one union centre, the farthest corner as the radius, scale = 0.92 × 128 / radius), applied once to all 72 drawings of `full-walk`, `full-hop-flight` and `full-wave-bow` together. Centre (147.5, 136.5) of the 256 px camera, radius 149.10, scale 0.7898, so the frame sits at left 4.493 %, top 7.887 % and 78.981 % of the slot square (`export-mascot.py` computes it and checks that the same rule reproduces `framing.ts` for all eight atlases; `inventory.json`, `amendment.sharedFraming`). The welcome frame (the empty conversation, 64 px) uses it too. So the figure keeps one size and place in every state and across every cut, whether or not the backing disc is visible, and never leaves the disc while airborne; nothing recentres at runtime. Measured at 32 px on the source cells and again on the rendered sheet (`§ Evidence`): every state the avatar rests on shows the same drawing; every drawing of the run, and the hop's first, keeps the figure's box within 1.4 px and its centroid within 0.52 px of the standing still's; only the flight leaves that spot, rising up to 3.1 px, by design. The wave's upright pose is the one exception, accepted as a property of the art (the coordinator, 2026-10-18): its box sits up to 2.0 px off the standing still's at the hand-over drawings (cells 0, 8 and 14 on the render) and up to 2.4 px over cells 0 to 14 of the source, while its centroid stays within 0.88 px; the art draws that pose lower and narrower, one framing cannot remove it, and no art is added. Outside the chat panel the page placements keep their per-atlas framing, unchanged. Two placements depart from the union-circle framing on purpose: the composer peek frames `stamp-peek` with its drawn wall on the slot's right edge (above), and in light mode, where no disc shows, a still in a reading column is placed by its ink box — the slot shifts left by the drawing's ink inset (idle 15.3 %, walk 15.6 %, error 15.3 % of the slot: 14.7, 15.0 and 14.7 px at 96; 9.8, 10.0 and 9.8 px at 64; the welcome frame's standing still 19.3 %, 12.4 px at 64) so the ink's left edge meets the column's text edge; in dark mode the disc is the box and the drawing stays centred in it (review A-11). The insets are measured by `export-mascot.py` (`inventory.json`, `ink.slotFractions`).

### The nine states

Timeline: a 24 Hz clock; a drawing may occupy several ticks (an exposure hold). Durations are computed from the sequences below (`ticks / 24`). "Still" is the atlas cell shown whenever motion is off at any level (`§ Motion controls`) and the drawing a one-shot clip rests on. Triggers name the chat signals in code today (JOURNEY SR-6) and, in brackets, the ones the implementation plan adds (frontend-only, brief Constraints). Text twins are final: Phase 5 revised them in place (plan carve-out 1); every change is in the revision index.

| State | Moment · trigger | Atlas · sequence (ticks) · duration | Exit | Interruption / reversal | Still (cell) | Text twin (final, Phase 5) · honest purpose |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `peek` | The first keystroke of a **focus session**: the student focused the composer by pointer or keyboard (the focus the app hands back after an answer starts no session), `botAnswering` is false, the newest avatar is still and the message region is at scroll end [adds a per-session typing flag with a 1.5 s expiry and scroll-end detection] | `stamp-peek` · hold 0 × 4, 0→23 at 2 each, hold 23 × 4 (56) · **2.33 s**, one-shot, rests fully out while typing continues; once per focus session | `retract` 1.5 s after the last keystroke (the flag's expiry) or on blur; **cut on send** (the slot empties at once, no retract: `walk` has priority); cut when the slash-command menu opens in its lane (N5) | Suppressed while the newest avatar is mid-clip or the region is scrolled up (and cut if either begins while it shows); after it has retracted or been cut it does not return until the student focuses the composer anew: the expiry never re-arms it, and an answer never re-arms it | 23 (fully out) | none: the student's own typing is the state · presence while composing (Norman, visceral; the brief's named exception); never a prompt to act |
| `retract` | The typing flag expired (1.5 s) or the composer blurred | `stamp-peek` · the exact reverse of `peek` from the drawing on screen (56 from fully out) · **2.33 s**, one-shot, ends hidden | the slot is empty (removed from layout once cell 0 is reached) until the next focus session | A send mid-retract cuts it (no reversal on send); new typing in the same session does not reverse it back (once per session) | 0 (a beak tip at the edge) | none · the companion steps back when attention moves |
| `walk` | Waiting for the first token from the send on, and every tool or agent step of the turn, before or after text (amended 2026-10-18): `botAnswering` true and the newest tutor message has `streaming === "null"`, `toolExecution.status === "running"` or a running agent step | `full-walk` in the shared framing · 0→23 at 2 each (48) · **2.0 s loop**, in place, from cell 0 at the send; one clock for the whole turn, so the loop never restarts between the wait, the steps and the streaming text; `full-hop-flight` is prefetched the moment it begins, so the first answer of a page load can hop (review A-6) | first token → `speaking` (the same loop: no change in the drawing); Stop → `idle`; error → `error`; offline → `offline` | Looping: cut at any exit but the first token, which changes the twin and not the drawing | the standing still (`full-hop-flight` 23) | "Thinking…" (the word, then the cycling dots as its ellipsis) while Kiwi waits for the first step or token; then the chain's header "Working…" and one label per step, which names the step and tells three kinds apart: a **reading** step starts with Checking, Looking up, Reading or Searching and changes nothing on the page ("Checking the page list", "Looking up your classes", "Reading this page", "Reading class analytics", "Searching your class materials"); an **acting** step names what Kiwi does and ends "for you", because it operates the page on the student's behalf ("Opening a page for you", "Opening class materials for you", "Clicking for you", "Filling in a field for you"); a **tool** step, where Kiwi runs one of its own tools away from the page, reads "Running code" or the tool's own name ("Search papers"), says "Using ⟨tool⟩…" on the tool pill, and never ends "for you", because it does not operate the page; a step with no name reads "Working…" (the labels are the SR-6 rows of the revision index, and `§ Voice and tone › Step labels` classifies each) · visibility of system status (Nielsen #1) while nothing can be read |
| `speaking` | Answer text is streaming: the newest message's `streaming` is mid-stream (`null`) and its text is growing [adds `isStreaming`] | the `walk` loop, continuing: no restart and no cut at the first token (the clock that started at the send carries on) · as long as text streams (amended 2026-10-18; it replaces the shallow dip of `portrait-nod-sleep`) | completion → `hop`; Stop → `idle`; error → `error`; offline → `offline` | Looping: cut at any exit; completion enters `hop` at the next tick from whatever run drawing is showing | the standing still (`full-hop-flight` 23) | the arriving text; the composer's reason reads "Kiwi is answering. Select Stop to interrupt." (Phase 3 composer rule) · "text is arriving": the run carries on until the answer is complete, so a pause in the stream never reads as the end (the user's decision, `§ Lock amendments`) |
| `hop` | Answer complete: `streaming` becomes `"stop"` with no error and no Stop [adds a user-abort flag] | `full-hop-flight` in the shared framing · 0→23 at **1 tick each**, hold 23 × 4 (28) · **1.17 s**, one-shot, entered at the next tick after completion from whatever run drawing is showing; it lands on 23, the standing still, so `idle` follows with no cut. All 24 drawings remain (crouch 0-3, take-off 4-8, flight 9-16, landing 17-23); dropping alternate drawings at 2 ticks would remove half the wing beats | `idle` when the clip ends (the same drawing: no cut) | Cut by a new send (`walk`), an error or offline; consecutive completions never stack (a hop in progress is finished or cut, never queued); the welcome row and older messages never hop; a like during the hop fills the thumb and plays no `wave`; a completion while the avatar is scrolled away or the tab is hidden plays no late hop, a deliberate difference from the library (the avatar shows the standing still when it returns) | 23 (landed: the standing still) | one polite live-region announcement "Kiwi finished answering.", then the focus move to the composer; visibly, the toolbar appears (Phase 3) · closure once per real completion (peak-end; Zeigarnik), identical every time, never a count |
| `wave` | The student likes the newest answer: Thumbs up in `Feedback.tsx` (`aria-pressed` turns true) | `full-wave-bow` in the shared framing · 0→14 at 3 each (the library's `wave` exposure), hold 14 × 6 (51) · **2.13 s**, one-shot → `idle` (the user's choice over the library's `heart`, a bow) | `idle` when the clip ends (a cut from cell 14 to the standing still) | Cut by a new send; a like while another answer streams or hops plays nothing (the thumb still fills, never queued); thumbs-down plays nothing and gets the same neutral acknowledgement (no sad clip, no guilt) | the standing still (motion off skips the wave); cell 8, the wing at its highest, is the sheet's inspection drawing (the notch between wing and body survives at 32 px on both grounds) | the pressed thumb (the filled glyph, named "Thumbs up" or "Thumbs down", the state carried by aria-pressed and never typed into the name) and one polite live-region announcement, "Feedback saved.", the same words for either thumb (no toast, Phase 3) · acknowledges the student's own action (Nielsen #1; the brief's named exception); never solicits a like |
| `error` | The newest message's `error` is set, or a stream error, or an error snackbar path. Three subtypes, one pose: **retryable** (a stream, network or server error: the error row with Retry); **quota or permission** (429 "out of tokens", 403 "not allowed": the error row with a diagnosis — what happened and who to ask — and no Retry); **backend error text delivered as a tutor bubble** ("Error: {raw message}", SR-6: the bubble is wrapped per Phase 5's raw-message rule, the message is classed `error`, never `hop`). A stream error is terminal: no further token renders, the message is marked `error`, no `hop` can follow, and the next event is Retry or a new send | none: the run cuts to the standing still (no full-body error drawing exists and none is invented; the error row carries the state) | Retry or a new send → `walk`; otherwise the still holds with the error row | An error mid-stream cuts `speaking` here and no `hop` follows; a second error restarts nothing (the still holds) | the standing still (`full-hop-flight` 23) | the error row, `role="alert"`: retryable → "Kiwi couldn't finish answering. {cause line} Try again." + Retry (secondary), the cause line being "It couldn't reach the server." (the closed line without its "Kiwi", which the sentence before already says), "The server returned an error ({status})." or "The server didn't respond as expected."; quota → "Kiwi can't answer right now. You've reached your usage limit. Ask your instructor or an administrator about it." and permission → "Kiwi can't answer right now. Your account can't use this feature. Ask an administrator for access.", and expired → "Couldn't send your question. Your sign-in has expired. Your question isn't saved. Copy it, then reload the page to sign in again." (the "No token" path: the sign-in is gone before the question is sent), none of the three with Retry; backend text → "Kiwi couldn't answer. The server said: \"{message}\". Try again." in a wrapped bubble (rule C10; the implementation plan maps codes and paths to subtypes, and `§ Voice and tone › Errors` supersedes the hand-off for "No token" and "Token expired") · an honest failure with a way out where one exists (Nielsen #9), apologetic not alarming |
| `offline` | The browser reports `navigator.onLine === false` (the `offline` event), or the chat library has been reconnecting ("Connection lost") for more than 2 s [adds online status] | none: the standing still (no full-body sleep drawing exists and none is invented; the composer's line carries the state) · on reconnect there is no wake clip: the pending state follows at once | `online` event plus one successful request or reconnect, held 2 s → the pending state: a restarted request runs `walk` from cell 0, a resumed stream `speaking`, otherwise `idle` (the same drawing) | Hysteresis both ways (2 s) so a flapping connection never flickers; entering offline cuts any clip and no `hop` follows a stream that died | the standing still (`full-hop-flight` 23) | the composer's status line in the lock's **warning status form** (`--status-warning-surface`, `--status-warning-text`, the triangle icon; offline is a condition the student may fix, not an error, so not the error form and not plain secondary text), `role="status"`, written once after the hysteresis: "You're offline. Waiting for your connection."; the field is disabled with that reason (`aria-describedby`); while an answer was pending Send has already become Stop (Phase 3) and Stop stays enabled, and the pending tutor row shows **static** text, "Paused while you're offline" (in compare mode, whose lanes give up and do not resume, "Stopped while you're offline"), never the in-progress dots · calibrated: "cannot answer now" |
| `idle` | Everything else: after `hop` (the same drawing), `wave`, an error or offline, after Stop, and on every older message and the welcome row | `full-hop-flight` cell 23 in the shared framing · **no sequence**: the standing still, the drawing the hop lands on (amended 2026-10-18; `portrait-blink` is no longer the avatar's) | a new send → `walk`; the first keystroke of a focus session → the composer peek (not the avatar) | none | the standing still (`full-hop-flight` 23) | none: the composer placeholder is the resting prompt · present and quiet (G9b) |

**The user's decisions (2026-10-17, on the rendered sheet).** `hop` lasts about 1 s: every drawing at 1 tick of the clock plus a 4-tick settle, 1.17 s (the gallery plays 3 ticks per drawing, 3.5 s; the brief's "~0.7 s" was a stale constant the library overwrites at load). `speaking` keeps the shallow dip. The like reaction is `wave` (2.13 s) instead of the library's `heart`, drawn as a bow (2.42 s as first specified). The companion sheet renders each beside what it replaced.

**The user's decisions (2026-10-18, on the companion sheet and the previews).** The tutor avatar keeps one small, full-body framing, and Kiwi runs until the answer is complete, then flies once (plan Decision Log, the two newest companion rows; `§ Lock amendments`). This supersedes the 2026-10-17 shallow dip for `speaking`, which was a portrait drawing: the run continues through the streaming text instead. The hop of about 1 s and the wave on a like stand as decided. The re-rendered companion sheet plays the amended sequence end to end at 32 px beside the avatar it replaces (`§ Evidence`).

**Precedence when events coincide:** send (`walk`) > offline > error > hop > wave > peek. A higher event cuts whatever lower clip is showing; a lower event that arrives while a higher one shows is dropped, never queued.

**Collisions (one clause each).** A tool or agent step that starts after narration text has streamed in the same turn: the run carries on with no restart (amended 2026-10-18; the step chain's "Running ⟨tool⟩…" carries the step). A like during `hop`: the thumb fills and the live region speaks; the hop finishes; `wave` does not play and is never queued. Motion off and a one-shot: `hop` and `wave`, which end in `idle`, are not shown at all (the avatar keeps the standing still; a one-second cut between two stills would be a flicker); `error` and `offline` rest on the standing still, so with motion off the avatar does not change at all. An atlas not yet loaded when its state is entered: the avatar shows the standing still until it arrives; the clip is never played late once the atlas lands, and a `hop` whose atlas was missing at completion is skipped, never queued — a fallback for a slow or failed fetch, since `full-hop-flight` is prefetched as `walk` begins. The 32 px avatar at rest (older rows, the newest at rest, the welcome row once a message exists) loads `stills/stand.64.webp` (1,920 B), the 2× standing still, and every state the avatar can rest on uses that one still.

**Compare mode (instructors and admins).** `ExperimentCompare` renders two message regions. One mover: only the avatar of the most recently started answer animates (the column whose first token arrived last; ties, as when one send starts both, go to column A); the other column's avatar holds its still and its text twins ("Thinking…", the arriving text) carry its state. No exception to the rule.

### Motion controls

Motion plays only when all three levels allow it; a higher level cannot be overridden by a lower one. With motion off at any level the avatar shows the standing still in every state (`hop` and `wave` are skipped, `§ The nine states`, the collisions), so it does not change at all, and the composer peek is not shown at all (its still would be an element popping in and out beside the composer: motion by another name, and G14 already says "there is no peek" for the flag). The text twins carry every state, as they always do (principle 4).

| Level | Control | Scope | Off means |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `VITE_ENABLE_MASCOT_MOTION` (build-time, read through `src/config/branding.ts`; G14) | the deployment | the avatar is the idle still on every row; no peek, walk, speaking, hop or wave; the Display menu **hides** the Kiwi animation switch; no atlas is ever fetched (stills only) |
| 2 | `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (the OS) | the device | stills; no peek; the Display menu draws the switch **inert**: `aria-disabled="true"`, focusable, with the reason "Your device asks for reduced motion" as its `aria-describedby` (Phase 3, `§ The Display menu`), never a native `disabled`; a stored choice is kept and applies again when the preference clears |
| 3 | The Display menu's **Kiwi animation** switch (G4; `localStorage`, per device; default on) | the device | stills; no peek; sign-in and register honour it (there is no top bar there) |

Also paused, at every level: a hidden tab and an offscreen avatar (the library's `useKiwiMotion`), so a long conversation scrolled past its newest message animates nothing, and a long answer that scrolls the running avatar out of view stops the run there. A loop resumes where it paused; a one-shot whose moment passes while its avatar is hidden or offscreen is not played late, and the avatar shows the standing still when it returns (amended 2026-10-18). This is a deliberate difference from the library, whose player starts such a clip from its first drawing when the avatar comes back: a hop marks the real completion or nothing (principle 5). The non-mascot motion budget (`§ Motion`) is unaffected by these controls.

### Placements

Every placement names its slot token, size from `md` and below `md` (390 px, G13), pose and backing; the same list is annotated on each page spec in `kiwi-ui/JOURNEY.md` (`Companion (Phase 4)` bullets). Only the two chat placements animate; every other slot loads one still (the 2× WebP of its size), never an atlas.

**Drawings (amended 2026-10-18).** Inside the chat panel, the avatar on every tutor row and the welcome frame, every pose is a full-body drawing in the avatar's shared framing, and at rest it is the standing still (`full-hop-flight` 23). The page placements outside it keep their current stills and their per-atlas framing on purpose, not by oversight: they are not the chat avatar sequence, they never animate and never sit beside the avatar, so the user's decision about the avatar does not reach them. In the table below, a page placement's idle still is the portrait (`portrait-blink` 0), its error still the lowered head (`portrait-nod-sleep` 8) and the walk still the standing bird (`full-walk` 0).

| Where (JOURNEY) | Slot | Size ≥ md / < md | Pose | Backing | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

### Backing and contrast

The forest outline `#234631` (artwork) must clear 3:1 against whatever is behind it (WCAG 1.4.11; brief done-when 2 names the mascot backing in the manual pass). Measured on the tokens (WCAG 2.x; `check-tokens.mjs` § 20, 7 required pairs, 0 below; all seven reproduced live by the companion sheet from computed styles):

| Mode | Behind the outline | Value | Ratio | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| dark | `--companion-backing` (light paper-2, frozen) | `#faf9f3` | 10.00:1 | PASS |
| light | `--surface` (the backing is transparent) | `#faf9f3` | 10.00:1 | PASS |
| light | `--background` | `#f4f1e4` | 9.31:1 | PASS |
| light | `--surface-hover` (the class-card cover tile) | `#f1f0ed` | 9.25:1 | PASS |
| light | `--surface-active` | `#e9e8e4` | 8.60:1 | PASS |
| light | `--accent-bg-subtle` | `#e6f5eb` | 9.35:1 | PASS |
| light | `--status-error-surface` (the error row) | `#ffebe9` | 9.19:1 | PASS |

**Pixel-sampled (review A-5).** Once anti-aliased and resampled, the artwork's colours are a hue family, not exact values, so `export-mascot.py` records in `inventory.json` the lightest forest-class pixel (alpha ≥ 128, the source's own mask rule) of every shipped asset, and `check-tokens.mjs` § 20 asserts that it still clears 3:1 on the dark backing and on the lowest light surface (`--surface-active`): since the 2026-10-18 amendment 21 assets (the 4 shipped atlases; the standing still at 64 and 128 px; the page placements' stills at 128 and 192 px; the wave, peek and retract stills at 64, 128 and 192 px), 0 below. The lightest forest pixels, which are edge blends, measure 4.38-6.77:1 on the backing and 3.77-5.82:1 on `--surface-active`; the solid forest 10.00:1 and 8.60:1 as in the table.

Informational: the backing disc against the dark canvas 16.64:1 and the dark sheet 15.08:1 (a visible disc by design, the lock's backing circle; its glare in a long dark conversation is checked in Phase 7); the tan bill 1.76:1 on the backing (the bill is not the outline; the forest silhouette carries the shape); without a backing the outline would measure 1.22-1.66:1 on the dark surfaces, which is why the disc exists (G9). The launcher glyph is `--accent-on-solid` on `--accent-solid`: 13.19:1 light, 10.06:1 dark (`§ Contrast`).

**Assistive technology and announcements.** Every mascot element is `aria-hidden="true"` with no role, name or title; the mascot is never a control (the peek and the avatar take no pointer events; the launcher is the button and its glyph is decorative). The text twins are what is announced, each once per event: the error row is `role="alert"` (assertive, announced when inserted; focus does not move, and Retry is the next tab stop); the composer's offline line (the warning status form above) is `role="status"` (polite), written once after the 2 s hysteresis and cleared on reconnect without an announcement (the next state's own text carries it); completion is one polite live-region announcement ("Kiwi finished answering."), after which, on the next frame, focus moves to the composer (Phase 3), which announces only its own name — the composer's description never repeats the completion text, so nothing is spoken twice; a like is one polite announcement ("Feedback saved.") beside the pressed thumb (`aria-pressed="true"`). No region repeats for the same event, and the mascot never adds an utterance of its own.

### Asset budget and port inventory

**Runtime assets in the source** (`public/kiwi-mascot/frames-v3/`, measured at the pinned commit; SHA-256 in `provenance.md`): `full-hop-flight.webp` 564,972 B · `full-walk.webp` 571,354 B · `full-wave-bow.webp` 496,314 B · `portrait-blink.webp` 688,748 B · `portrait-doze.webp` 595,390 B · `portrait-nod-sleep.webp` 599,782 B · `portrait-turn.webp` 597,534 B · `stamp-peek.webp` 343,046 B; **4,457,140 B** in all, 256 px cells. Not ported: `frames-v2/`, the lossless PNG twins, the `*.source.png`, the per-frame PNG directories, the animated `kiwi-<state>.webp` previews, the v1 SVG rigs, `KiwiLoader`, `KiwiStamp` as the launcher. Ported for playback since the 2026-10-18 amendment: four atlases, `full-walk`, `full-hop-flight`, `full-wave-bow` and `stamp-peek`; `portrait-blink` and `portrait-nod-sleep` supply page stills only (the portrait and the lowered head), and `portrait-doze` and `portrait-turn` are not ported.

**Cell size per placement and what is present at each state.** No 256 px atlas is loaded for a 32 px avatar (brief done-when 7): the port generates the resampled atlases at build time (`export-mascot.py` is the reference; lossless WebP, Lanczos, 6 × 4 cells), measured below.

| Placement | Cell | Atlases present (state) | Bytes each |
|---|---|---|---|
| tutor avatar 32 px | 64 (2×) | `full-walk` (walk and speaking: one loop) 34,942 · `full-hop-flight` (hop) 34,622 · `full-wave-bow` (wave) 34,430 | 103,994 B for the three; every resting state is the standing still and the peek is its own slot |
| composer peek 48 px | 96 (2×) | `stamp-peek` (peek, retract) | 60,786 B |
| every still slot (96 / 64 / the 32 px avatar at rest) | one still, 192 / 128 / 64 px WebP | the standing still — / 5,250 / 1,920 (the welcome frame; the avatar at rest) · the page placements' `idle` 14,776 / 8,130 / — · `error` 11,988 / 6,326 / — · `walk` 10,028 / 5,140 / — | 2-15 KB each; no atlas |
| launcher | SVG | `launcher-glyph.svg` | 1,752 B |

**Loading order (lazy, per state, with one prefetch).** An atlas is fetched the first time its state is entered, except that `full-hop-flight` is prefetched the moment `walk` begins (review A-6): nothing else is in flight then, and the first answer of a page load can hop; no portrait atlas is fetched for the avatar (amended 2026-10-18). Stills are fetched where they render. A chat page therefore loads, before the first answer completes: the welcome frame's standing still at 128 px (5,250 B), the welcome row's standing still at 64 px once a message exists (1,920 B), then `full-walk` + `full-hop-flight` (69,564 B) = **76,734 B** (the prefetch moves the second atlas earlier, it adds nothing); with `stamp-peek` at 96 px, if the student typed, **137,520 B**. **Budget: ≤ 300 KB** (brief done-when 7), met with a 162 KB margin. `full-wave-bow` loads only on a like. **The whole shipped set** is the three avatar atlases at 64 px (103,994 B) + `stamp-peek` at 96 px (60,786 B) + the stills the placements load (63,558 B: the standing still at 64 and 128 px; the page placements' 128 px idle, error and walk and 192 px idle, walk and error) + the launcher glyph (1,752 B) = **230,090 B**. With motion off at level 1 nothing but stills and the glyph is ever fetched. The 128 px set in `atlas/` is the companion sheet's inspection size and is not shipped; 3× phones draw the 64 px cells at 1.5× (N7), accepted for a 32 px decorative drawing.

### Edge cases (plan)

- **Generation stopped by the student** (Stop, Phase 3): `walk` or `speaking` cuts to `idle`; no `hop`; the partial answer stays.
- **Error mid-stream:** `speaking` cuts to `error`; no `hop`; the error row (`role="alert"`) and Retry appear under the partial text.
- **Rapid consecutive answers:** a hop is finished or cut by the next `walk`, never queued; a completion that arrives while a hop plays (compare mode's other column) does not restart it.
- **Flapping offline signal:** 2 s hysteresis on entry and on exit; a reconnect under 2 s shows nothing and announces nothing.
- **Send while the peek shows:** the peek is cut, no retract (`walk` has priority); the focus session is consumed. Phase 3 disables the composer while Kiwi answers, so typing cannot start mid-stream; the flag's expiry never re-arms the peek, and the focus the app returns after the answer starts no session, so the peek does not return until the student focuses the composer again.
- **Peek after an answer (the consequence):** because the focus the app returns after an answer starts no session, a student who keeps the composer focused for a whole sitting sees the peek once, at their first keystroke, and not again until they click or tab into the composer anew. That quiet is intended.
- **Scrolled up while typing:** the peek is suppressed (and cut if it was showing), so the lane's inner half is always the region's empty bottom padding; it never overlays text.
- **The slash-command menu** (a bare `/`) opens in the lane: the peek is cut and does not return in that session (N5).
- **390 px:** the placements table's second size column; the panel is fullscreen and the composer full width, the peek keeps 48 px.
- **Screen readers:** the mascot is `aria-hidden`; the announcements are listed under `§ Backing and contrast`, one per event.
- **Hidden tab or scrolled-away avatar:** a loop pauses and resumes in phase (the library); a one-shot whose moment passes while its avatar is hidden is not played late (amended 2026-10-18; a deliberate difference from the library). A long answer scrolls the running avatar out of view, so the run pauses there and nothing moves beside the text the student is reading.
- **Compare mode:** one mover, the most recently started answer's avatar (`§ The nine states`).
- **Text twins are final:** Phase 5 revised them in place; `§ Voice and tone › Companion text` lists the new strings and what stays open (A-7).

### Ethics pass (deceptive-patterns, behavioral)

Walked against Brignull's nine categories (`dark-patterns.md`) and the six honest-use tests (`ethical-application.md`). Finding: **none present**.

| Category | Checked | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Urgency and false scarcity | no timer, countdown, "Kiwi is waiting", "last chance" | none: the companion never asks the student to hurry or return |
| 2 Misdirection | the mascot never sits beside a destructive or consequential control and never draws attention from Stop, Retry or the thumbs; it takes no pointer events; the peek yields to the slash menu | none |
| 3 Hidden information | nothing is disclosed through the mascot alone (never the only signal) | none |
| 4 Friction asymmetry | the Kiwi animation switch is one control, on and off alike, with the same neutral label in both positions; reduced motion is honoured without a setting | none (symmetry test: pass) |
| 5 Social manipulation | no activity counters, no "other students liked this", no fabricated reactions | none |
| 6 Default exploitation | the switch defaults to on for a decorative, reversible preference; the OS preference overrides it; nothing financial or privacy-related rides on it | none (user-beneficial default, transparent choice) |
| 7 Language manipulation | the switch reads "Kiwi animation: on / off"; no confirmshaming label on the decline; Phase 5 keeps every state text plain | none |
| 8 Addiction traps | no streak, no variable reward (every hop is identical and deterministic; the wave plays once per like and never for a dislike), no "come back" nudge, no dozing-when-ignored, no notification state; the peek shows once per focus session and never unprompted | none (internal-trigger test: the student's own question; reflective-level test: the student finished reading, nothing pulled them back) |
| 9 Pricing opacity | not applicable | none |

**Re-checked for the 2026-10-18 amendment.** The run that now lasts the whole answer is the system's own activity: in place, with no track, count or countdown, and it stops the moment the work stops, so it creates no urgency and claims no progress (category 1; `KiwiLoader`'s goal-gradient stays unused). It asks nothing of the student and pulls no one back, and the hop stays identical every time (category 8). Error and offline no longer have a pose of their own: the avatar stands and the words carry the state, so nothing in the drawing can read as blame or sadness. The cost is attention, a moving figure beside text being read; the user chose it, and one mover, reduced motion, the switch and the offscreen pause bound it. Finding: still none present.

**Data.** The typing flag is local: a boolean and a timestamp in the composer's component state; no keystroke content or timing leaves the browser, and the peek reacts to that local flag only (the brief's frontend-only constraint).

Each reaction's honest purpose is stated in the state table's last column; the test for every one of them is that it serves the student's own goal (reading and understanding the answer) and never the product's engagement metric (goal-alignment test: pass).
