### Hand-offs

- **Phase 5 (Words):** the text twins in the state table (drafts when handed over, final since Phase 5); the welcome frame's line; the offline line and the pending row's static text (handed over as "Paused: no connection.", now "Paused while you're offline"); the three error subtypes' copy (retryable with Retry; quota or permission with a diagnosis; backend text, wrapped); the `walk` twins that tell a reading step from an acting step; the three live-region strings (completion, feedback, the offline status); the Display menu's inert-switch reason.
- **Phase 7 (Final mocks):** the chat mock shows every state from this table as a frame strip with the one-mover rule visible; mascot frames are the stills in `.design-foundations/mocks/assets/mascot/`; the companion sheet is the motion reference; the offline-while-pending frame (Stop enabled, the offline line) is the one to reuse.
- **Implementation plan (classification and sizes):** map today's error paths to the three subtypes (SR-6: the `No token`, `Token expired`, `An error occurred`, `Server error` and `Connection lost` paths and a stream error → retryable; 429 "out of tokens" and 403 "not allowed" → quota or permission; `Error: {raw message}` bubbles → backend text); prefetch `full-hop-flight` when `walk` begins (no portrait atlas, amended 2026-10-18); the launcher takes its glyph box from `--companion-slot-launcher` (28 px) and `--launcher-icon-size` (Phase 3's `component.launcher.icon-size`, the 24 px stand-in) is retired for the launcher — marked `$deprecated` in the DTCG source, still emitted, and removed per the deprecation policy (review A-10).
- **Implementation plan:** port `src/components/KiwiMascot/` (player, framing, motion hooks) without `KiwiLoader` and without the launcher stamp; generate the 64 px and 96 px atlases and the 64 / 128 / 192 px stills at build time from the source atlases (`export-mascot.py` is the reference procedure); add the missing chat signals (a per-focus-session typing flag with a 1.5 s expiry, scroll-end detection on the message region, `isStreaming`, online status with 2 s hysteresis, a user-abort flag) in `ChatCore.tsx`; unit tests per chat event → state transition (brief done-when 7), including the send cut, the once-per-session peek and the compare-mode mover; `aria-hidden` on every mascot; `role="alert"` on the error row, `role="status"` on the offline line, the two polite live regions; the Display menu wiring per `§ The Display menu` (the switch `aria-disabled` + `aria-describedby` under reduced motion, hidden with the flag off); `VITE_ENABLE_MASCOT_MOTION` through `src/config/branding.ts` (G14); the launcher glyph at 28 px from `launcher-glyph.svg`.
- **Phase 7 (amended 2026-10-18):** the chat mock draws the avatar with the standing still and the shared framing in every state, and the welcome frame with the standing still at 64 px; the companion sheet's first section is the motion reference for the sequence, and `screenshots/companion-sequence.32.gif` its preview.
- **Implementation plan (amended 2026-10-18):** one framing for `full-walk`, `full-hop-flight` and `full-wave-bow` in the avatar and the welcome frame (left 4.493 %, top 7.887 %, 78.981 % of the slot; `inventory.json`, `amendment.sharedFraming`) in place of `framing.ts`'s per-atlas values there; the page placements keep `framing.ts`; `walk` and `speaking` share one clip whose clock starts at the send and is never reset by the first token or a step; `hop` starts at the next tick after completion from the drawing on screen and ends on cell 23, which is also the idle still (`stills/stand.64.webp`); a one-shot entered while the avatar is hidden or offscreen is not played (a deliberate difference from the library's player, which would play it late); no portrait atlas is ported for the avatar; unit tests for the no-restart rule, the next-tick entry and the late-one-shot rule.

## Voice and tone

**Phase 5 (2026-10-17), appended under the lock.** The words of the interface: one voice in two registers, six tones, eighteen rules, one vocabulary, the formats, the error anatomy, and the final wording of everything the design system and the companion left as a placeholder or a draft. It adds no token and changes no locked value or Phase 2 or Phase 3 wording; its touches on an earlier section are the Companion's text-twin column, which the plan's carve-out 1 lets it revise in place (`§ Companion › The nine states`), and, after the first review, principle 4's wording, the line that called the twins drafts and the draft wording of the Phase 5 hand-off, after the second, the one phrase of the Phase 4 intro that said which states have a text twin, and, after the seventh, the one sentence of the state-table intro that still called them drafts. Plan: `.design-foundations/plans/2026-10-16-kiwi-ui-stage-1.md`, Phase 5 (DW-5.1 to DW-5.7); brief G12. Doctrine applied: content-design (Podmajersky's voice chart and terminology agreement; Yifrah's error, button and confirmation patterns; Redish's plain, front-loaded, scannable text; Richards's start from the reader's need; Metts and Welfle's words as design material) and usability (Nielsen 1, 2, 4, 5, 6 and 9; Postel and Tesler for the error causes; peak-end for empty and completion states). Principle-derived choices are marked `[principle-derived]`.

**Where each part lives.** This section is the law for words. `kiwi-ui/JOURNEY.md` applies it route by route: each page spec carries a `Copy (Phase 5)` bullet (old → new, the rules, a one-line rationale), and `## Revision index` lists every changed string with its anchor, its rules and the tests that mention it. The shared strings below, the page bullets and the index are generated from one data set (`.design-foundations/copy/revisions/`), and `node .design-foundations/copy/check-copy.mjs` re-reads the source, lints every new string and fails on any drift (`§ Evidence`); `node .design-foundations/copy/prove-checks.mjs` plants a defect for each lint to prove that it fails, and `§ Rule coverage` says which lint covers which rule and what is checked by hand.

### Voice

The product has one voice; the register changes with the reader (Podmajersky). A voice that lists adjectives does not constrain, so every attribute below carries an in-range and an out-of-range example and the rules of language that produce it. In-range examples are strings from the revision index or sentences written to the same rules; out-of-range examples are what the rules rule out, and several ("Are you sure?", "Failed to load") are the product's old strings.

**The floor, for both audiences.** No exclamation mark, no emoji, no hype ("AI-powered", "seamless", "unlock"), no pun, no "we", "our" or "I" in product chrome, no claim the product cannot back, no developer text, institution and support details only from branding config (`src/config/branding.ts`), and the same word for the same thing everywhere. The tutor's generated answers and the class instructions are not covered: they are the model's and the instructor's.

#### Students

The student reads long answers, switches between chat, materials and code, and is often mid-task. The register is measured and precise, and warm only by being clear.

| Attribute | What it means | In range | Out of range | Rules of language |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Plain** | Everyday words, short sentences, the concrete noun. | "Choose a class to start." · "Kiwi is reading this page." | "Initiate a session." · "Kiwi is leveraging your course corpus." | Words a first-year uses; one idea per sentence; a term the student may not know is defined once, beside its first use (Richards); digits for numbers. |
| **Specific** | Says what is happening, what changed or what to do, with the object named. | "Searching your class materials" · "3 students removed" · "Select a class." | "Processing…" · "Done." · "Get started." | A verb and its object; the action or the fact first (Redish); every state has words. |
| **Steady** | Calm in every state: warm by being clear and reassuring, never excited or alarmed. | "Kiwi couldn't finish answering. The server didn't respond as expected. Try again." · "Your changes are still in the form." | "Oops! Something went wrong!" · "Hurry, your session is about to expire." | No exclamation marks, emoji, urgency or humour; an apology only where Kiwi is at fault; say what is safe (Yifrah). |
| **Candid about the AI** | Claims only what the product does, and says what Kiwi did or could not do. | "Ask about your class materials or your code." · "Kiwi repeated this step. It may be stuck." (shown when the same tool step runs more than twice in a row: `AgentStepChain.tsx:206` decides, `AgentStepChain.tsx:354-359` draws it) | "AI-powered learning." · "Kiwi knows everything about your course." | Kiwi in the third person; no claim about understanding, feeling or accuracy; "Thinking…" stays, for the wait before the first step only, because it is the user's own wording (G9) and is the one concession: once a step runs, its label names the step (`§ Step labels`). `[principle-derived: Phase 4's "calibrated" applied to words]` |

#### Instructors and admins

Staff work in dense tables and forms and make changes that affect other people. The register is operational and exact.

| Attribute | What it means | In range | Out of range | Rules of language |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Exact** | Names things as the screen names them; states counts, units and places. | "3 users removed from the roster" · "Roster: 12 users (10 active, 2 pending)" | "Users updated." · "Manage stuff." | One word per concept (C6); the sidebar label is the page title (C7); "{n} of {m}"; a noun on screen is the noun in the message. |
| **Direct** | Says what a control does and what happens next, verb first. | "Enroll students" · "Test connection" · "Approve event" | "Let's get your class started!" · "Take the next step." | An imperative for an action; no filler ("management", "easily", "simply"); one sentence where one will do. |
| **Consequence-first** | Before a change or a removal, says what will happen, to what, and whether it can be undone. | "Anything using it stops working, and this can't be undone." · "Students lose access to \"{name}\" in this class." | "Are you sure?" · "This action cannot be undone." | The consequence, then permanence, then the way out (Yifrah); the confirming button repeats the verb and the object (C11). |
| **Forthright about state** | Says what failed or only partly worked, how many, and what to do; never a silent failure. | "Approved 8 of 10 events. The server didn't respond as expected for the other 2. Refresh the list and try those again." | "Bulk approval completed." · "Something went wrong." | Counts for a partial result; a cause line from the closed set (C9); what is safe; raw server text quoted after Kiwi's own sentence (C10). |

### Tone map

Voice is stable; tone follows the moment (Podmajersky). The six moments below are the plan's list; a warning or the offline condition takes the waiting tone (a state that will resolve) or the error tone (one that needs the reader). What happened comes first, as the error formula has it, and the tone follows in the same line: "You're offline. Waiting for your connection." names the condition, then says that it will resolve.

| Moment | Tone | What shifts | Students | Instructors and admins | Never |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Error** | Calm and precise; apologetic only when Kiwi is at fault. | Personality drops, precision rises; what happened, why, what to do; says what is safe. | "Kiwi couldn't finish answering. The server didn't respond as expected. Try again." | "Couldn't save the class. The server didn't respond as expected. Your changes are still in the form. Check the details and try again." | "Oops", "Failed to", an exclamation mark, a code on its own, blame. |
| **Success** | Quiet: one line, in the past tense. | Warmth comes from acknowledging the act, not from celebrating it; a count where there is one; Undo where it can be undone. | "Feedback saved." (announced) · "Copied" | "3 users removed from the roster" · "Webhook created" | "Success!", "Awesome", "successfully". |
| **Empty** | Welcoming and forward-looking, low pressure. | The warmest register in the product, with the mascot beside it; says what goes here and the first step, or who acts. | "No classes yet" with "Your instructor or an administrator adds you to a class. It will appear here when they do." | "No classes yet" with "Create the first class to get started." | "Nothing here", "No results found", urgency. |
| **Waiting** | Steady and present: names what is happening. | An -ing verb and its object; no personality; never a made-up percentage or an unmeasured duration. The wait before the first step says "Thinking…", the one concession (`§ Voice`); once a step runs, its label names the step (`§ Step labels`). | "Thinking…" (the wait) · "Searching your class materials" (a step) | "Analyzing student questions…" | "Hang tight!", a fake progress figure, a duration nobody measured. |
| **Sign-in** | Welcoming and brief; the one place the product says what it is. | The display line states the task; the one sub-line under it says what to enter (S-05.02); the page's aside says what Kiwi does in one line (SR-5.01); the mascot sits beside it. | "Sign in to Kiwi" with "Use the email address linked to your Kiwi account." | The same page and the same line for every role: "Sign in to Kiwi" with "Use the email address linked to your Kiwi account." | A three-beat slogan, "AI-powered", a greeting that assumes a returning user. |
| **Destructive confirmation** | Serious and specific; no humour. | The title asks the question; the body gives the consequence, then permanence; the button repeats the verb and the object. A grant of access (S-08) takes the same form. | "Delete this chat?" with "Its messages are deleted. This can't be undone." and "Delete chat" (the pattern for the first destructive action a student gets; none exists today) | "Delete this file?" with "\"{fileName}\" is deleted, along with its comments, its summaries, the search index Kiwi built from it and the stored file. Chats that cited it keep their answers. You can't undo this." and "Delete file". | "Are you sure?", "Yes", "OK", a bare "Delete". |

### Microcopy rules

Each revision in the index names the rules it applies by number. A wrong → right pair is a real string from the product.

| Rule | Name | The rule | Principle | Wrong → right |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `C1` | Case | Sentence case for headings, buttons, labels, tabs, menu items, column headers, chips, tooltips and dialog titles: a capital on the first word, on proper nouns and acronyms (Kiwi, Google, OAuth, MCP, PDF, API, CSV, SSO, ID, URL, JSON, Python, R, ChatGPT, Qwen) and on the name of a control or page that a sentence points to, spelled as the screen spells it ("Select Retry", "the Experiments page"). Capitals are never typed into a string and never drawn by CSS (`§ Never`). | Redish (scanning); Nielsen #4; the lock | "Create New Class" → "Create class" · "ENROLL USERS" → "Enroll students" |
| `C2` | Buttons | A button is a verb and an object that name the outcome. Never "Submit", "OK", "Yes" or "Confirm" alone; the exit is "Cancel"; a confirming button repeats the verb and the object. One line at 1280 px, so three words where they fit (TD-34) and five at most (`check-copy.mjs` C2-A). **The carve-out is closed.** Seven conventional single-verb controls, whose object is clear from where they sit, stand alone: Retry, Dismiss, Undo, Stop, Copy, Apply and Refresh. So do the two exits, Cancel and Close, and the two conventional account controls, Sign in and Sign out, whose object is the account; the words Phase 3's component tables already give the PDF toolbar and its search panel (First, Previous, Next, Last, Find, Search, Thumbnails, Fullscreen); and a control that names its state, not an action (the sort-direction toggle's Ascending and Descending, a disabled button that reads Registered). Every other button names its object ("Edit expiration", "Save comment", "Activate experiment"). A two-word label whose second word is a particle or a quantifier names no object ("Select all", "Approve selected", "Try again"), so it names one ("Select all recipients", "Approve selected events"). `check-copy.mjs` (H12) reads every label a button can show, two-word verb-only ones included, and B1 every New one. | Yifrah; Nielsen #2 | "Submit" → "Save as draft" · "Delete" → "Deactivate webhook" |
| `C3` | Dates and times | One format, whatever the browser locale (`§ Dates, times and numbers`): `D MMM YYYY`, with `, HH:mm` for a date and time and `HH:mm` for a time. The variants are the table's and no others: seconds (`:ss`) on an audit log; a range, `D MMM – D MMM YYYY`, whose year is written twice when the years differ ("28 Dec 2025 – 3 Jan 2026"); ISO dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`) in machine files, in the file name and inside the file; the calendar's month heading (`MMMM YYYY`, "October 2026"); and the accessible long forms (a day's name, `D MMMM YYYY`; a weekday's full name). | Nielsen #4; G12 | "12月31日" → "31 Dec 2026" |
| `C4` | Numbers and plurals | Digits; a comma thousands separator from 1,000; a real singular and plural for every count, written as `{n: count student}` in a string of this guide (never "student(s)", never "1 student, or {n} students", never a bare `{n}` or other count slot before a plural noun, directly or one word before it, as in `{n} events` or `{n} selected users`, which read "1 events" when n is 1: a note that gives the singular reading does not excuse the string, so `check-copy.mjs` D3, H10 and H17 fail on it with or without one; never a slot that names two values: a value that alternates is one row per value, each saying when it shows); a unit written one way (`min` for minutes, never "minutes" or "mins"; hours, days and seconds through the helper, `{n: count day}`), and digits for every number ("2 variants", not "two variants"; `check-copy.mjs` C4-B). | Redish; Nielsen #4 | "1 classes" → "1 class" · "{n} event(s) selected" → "{n: count event} selected" |
| `C5` | Marks | No exclamation marks anywhere. The ellipsis character for progress, never three full stops. No emoji. "and", not "&". Straight quotes. No em dashes in interface copy, and no dash of any kind in a sentence. **Two dashes are kept.** The first is the glyph for an empty value, a lone "—" in a cell or a field that has nothing to show, or "— / —" for the page indicator's two values (DS.53). It is a glyph, not punctuation; it is the only glyph for "no value", never "-", an en dash or "N/A" for the same meaning; and each one carries the accessible name "No value" (DS.54), because a screen reader skips a lone dash or reads it as "dash". The second is the en dash in a date or number range ("1 Oct – 7 Oct 2026", as the range row of `§ Dates, times and numbers` writes it, or "3–5"): between two dates or two numbers and nowhere else, and `check-copy.mjs` fails on an en dash in any other place. **End punctuation:** names (labels, buttons, headings, tabs, chips, tooltips, placeholders, column headers) take none, and a name takes no colon, but a caption that introduces its value or a list on the same line keeps one ("Last used: 17 Oct 2026", "Welcome email (for 3 pending users):"; each such caption is a written exemption); a one-clause result of 8 words or fewer, such as a toast, takes none; so does an empty line that reports an absence in one clause of 8 words or fewer ("No classes yet", "No classes assigned to you yet", "Nothing matches these filters"), as the empty-state heading does (DS.23); a heading or dialog title that asks a question ends with its question mark (the confirmation titles of C11, "Deactivate this webhook?", and the access heading on S-08, "Allow access to your account?"); every other sentence ends with a full stop, a question mark or an ellipsis (a disabled reason is a sentence and keeps its full stop, and so does the explanation or instruction under an empty heading, "Create the first class to get started."); a live-region announcement is a full sentence. | Yifrah; the lock's copy tells | "Welcome to Kiwi!" → "Your classes" · "Loading..." → "Loading…" · "Login & Authorize" → "Sign in and allow access" · "No materials yet." → "No materials yet" · "-" → "—" (an empty value) |
| `C6` | Terminology | One word per concept (`§ Terminology`). | Podmajersky; Nielsen #4 | "Whitelist" → "Allowlist" · "Kiwi Platform" → "Kiwi" |
| `C7` | Page names | The sidebar label is the page title, and the same words name the page everywhere (`§ Page names`). | Nielsen #4; Redish; TD-21 | "System Email" beside "Email Management" → "System email" beside "System email" |
| `C8` | Person | "You" for the reader; "Kiwi" in the third person for the product and the tutor; never "we", "our", "let's" or "I" in chrome (a button the person speaks, such as "I've copied the secret", is their own voice), and "Your", never "My", for what belongs to the reader ("Your classes"); "please" only to ask for real effort, never in an error. | Podmajersky; Yifrah | "Please contact us at" → "Contact {SUPPORT_EMAIL}" · "I'll draft the config" → "The assistant drafts the setup" |
| `C9` | Errors | What happened, why, what to do (`§ Errors`), with "Your changes are still in the form." between the why and the what to do where the page keeps what was typed. Never blame, never a dead end, never "Failed to", never "Oops". | Yifrah; Nielsen #9 | "Failed to load MCP servers" → "Couldn't load MCP servers. The server didn't respond as expected. Select Refresh." |
| `C10` | Raw text | Server text and developer text are never the whole message: Kiwi's sentence comes first, the server's words follow in quotes, wrapped and clamped, the clamp a focusable element whose accessible description is the full text, with a visible "Show full message" button on a touch screen as well (DS.51, `§ Errors`); an environment variable, a backend instruction or a stack never appears, and the backend writes any message that may be quoted for the person who reads it (`§ Hand-offs`). | Nielsen #9; Richards | "Backend error: … This is a known issue" → "The server needs user authentication set up for webhooks. Ask whoever maintains this Kiwi deployment to fix it." |
| `C11` | Confirmations | A title that asks the question; a body that gives the consequence, then permanence, then the way out; a button that repeats the verb and the object. Never "Are you sure?" A reversible action gets an Undo toast instead of a dialog. | Yifrah stakes-clarity; Nielsen #3 and #5 | "Are you sure you want to delete this webhook? This action cannot be undone." → "Deactivate this webhook?" · "The webhook stops receiving events and moves to the Inactive tab. You can turn it on again there: select Edit webhook and set Status to Active." |
| `C12` | Empty states | First use: what goes here and the first step, with one primary button for a role that can act. No action: the copy says who acts and when, and there is no button (never a disabled one). No results: names what was searched. Load error: an error, not an empty state. | Redish; NN/g empty-state types; peak-end | "No classes found" → "No classes yet" · "No classes match \"{search}\"" |
| `C13` | Loading and progress | An -ing verb and its object, then the ellipsis character; the object is named when known; a one-clause progress line ends with the ellipsis, never a full stop; never a made-up percentage. | Nielsen #1; Doherty | "Loading sessions..." → "Loading chats…" · "Navigating" → "Opening a page for you" |
| `C14` | Labels, helpers, placeholders | A label is visible and persistent and says what the field is; helper text adds information (a format, a consequence, the default) and never repeats the label; a placeholder is an example, never an instruction or a second label (the one exception is a search box or a message box that has no label of its own: its placeholder says what the box does, "Search classes", "Ask a question, or type / for commands", "Describe your experiment…", and, because the box has no other label, the same words are its `aria-label`); an optional field says "(optional)"; no asterisk is typed into a label. A line that points at a place says where in words, never by a colour or a shape: "Each failed row says why.", not "Each red row says why" (WCAG 1.3.3 and 1.4.1; the lock's rule that colour is never the only signal), and `check-copy.mjs` C14-C fails on a colour used as a pointer. | Redish; WCAG 3.3.2, 1.3.3, 1.4.1; Yifrah | "Enter the name of the class for this announcement" → "Shown in the subject and the email header." · "Each red row says why" → "Each failed row says why." |
| `C15` | Icon-only names | A verb phrase, the same in the tooltip and the `aria-label`, naming its object when a row repeats the control; a toggle's state is `aria-pressed`, not part of the name; a disabled control states its own reason (`§ Components`). | Nielsen #6; WCAG 4.1.2 | "close" → "Close chat" · "" → "Send message" |
| `C16` | Claims | Nothing about the AI the product cannot back; nothing the page does not do (a placeholder action never reports success); no developer text. | G12; ai-native: calibrate | "Continue learning with AI-powered support." → "Use the email address linked to your Kiwi account." |
| `C17` | Branding | An institution's name, logo text and support address come only from `src/config/branding.ts` (`INSTITUTION_NAME`, `BRAND_LABEL`, `SUPPORT_EMAIL`); a line that names the support address is omitted when it is empty. | The plan's constraint | "…built for the {INSTITUTION_NAME} community" → "Kiwi is an AI tutor for classes at {INSTITUTION_NAME}." |
| `C18` | Spelling | American English: "enroll", "analyze", "canceled". The design documents may spell otherwise; strings do not. | Nielsen #4 | (the strings already read this way) |

### Dates, times and numbers

The browser's locale decides today (`toLocaleString()` and its siblings with the default locale at 39 call sites in 20 files, TD-24: "12月31日" under zh-CN, "2026/10/14 01:19:43" in the app registry). One format replaces it.

| What | Format | Example | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Date | `D MMM YYYY` | 17 Oct 2026 | Day without a leading zero; the year always. |
| Date and time | `D MMM YYYY, HH:mm` | 17 Oct 2026, 14:05 | 24-hour clock; no seconds unless the surface is an audit log, which adds `:ss`. |
| Time | `HH:mm` | 14:05 | |
| Range | `D MMM – D MMM YYYY` | 1 Oct – 7 Oct 2026 | An en dash; the year once at the end, twice if the years differ ("28 Dec 2025 – 3 Jan 2026"). The analytics period button shows it for a custom range, and its accessible name repeats it; a preset shows its own label ("Last 7 days"). |
| Calendar header | `MMMM YYYY` | October 2026 | The full month name and the year; the grid's accessible name is the same. |
| Calendar day (accessible name) | `D MMMM YYYY` | 17 October 2026 | Day first, with the full month name; the cell shows the day number. |
| Weekday heading | the first letter of the weekday's name | S M T W T F S | The accessible name is the full name, Sunday to Saturday. |
| Count | `1,234` | 1,234 students | A comma grouping from 1,000, from a fixed `en-US` format. |
| Count with a noun | `{n: count student}` | 1 student, 2 students, 0 students, 1,234 students | One helper picks the noun's form: the singular when the number is exactly 1, the plural otherwise (regular endings only: s, es after s, x, z, ch and sh, ies after a consonant and y; a fraction takes the plural, "1.5 hours"). A noun phrase takes the plural on its last word (`{n: count matching page}` reads 1 matching page, 3 matching pages). A row never writes a bare `{n}` before the noun: `{n} students` reads "1 students" when n is 1, and no note excuses it (D3). Where the noun follows a total, the helper goes on the total: `{n} of {m: count class}` reads "1 of 1 class" and "2 of 3 classes". |
| Machine files | `YYYY-MM-DD` | `allowlist_2026-10-17.csv` | ISO, in the file name and inside the file. |

The time zone is the viewer's own and is never converted by locale. Where a timestamp could be misread (audit logs, expirations, the analytics range) the surface names the zone: the range picker already captions "Local ({timeZone})", and a table column says "(local time)". Month names come from one fixed table (Jan, Feb, Mar, Apr, May, Jun, Jul, Aug, Sep, Oct, Nov, Dec), never from the browser: ICU spells September "Sept" in some locales. The calendar's full month names (January to December) and weekday names (Sunday to Saturday) come from the same table, written out in the component: date-fns and the browser do the date arithmetic only, and no `format()` pattern with month or weekday names (`MMM`, `MMMM`, `EEE`), no `locale` option and no `toLocale*String()` draws a date or a time (`check-copy.mjs` H2 fails on any that does; the range picker's `locale` prop goes away). The implementation is one helper pair (`formatDate`, `formatDateTime`) and one number formatter, and a guard sibling to the raw-colour guard that fails on a `toLocale*String()` call with no locale and on one with any explicit locale other than the fixed English one, in `src/` outside generated code (`timeUtils.ts:4` passes `'zh-CN'`; its only caller is the orphaned `StudentProblemView.tsx`, so it goes with the orphans, and the chart axis helper's four `en-US` display formats are Phase 6's, named in its hand-off; `analyticsDateRangePresets.ts:24` reads a zone's date parts as numbers and draws nothing, and `SessionDaily.tsx:40` is an orphan); the revision index lists every call site by anchor.

**The date control (`DateTimeField`).** The browser draws a native date, date-time or time input in its own locale (month first and a 12-hour clock under en-US; "2026/10/14" or "12月31日" elsewhere), and a page can neither reformat nor restyle it, so Stage 1 uses none. The native controls listed below become one component, `DateTimeField`: a text field with an explicit format and a calendar popover, in three modes. It is a component reference in the style of `§ Inputs, selects and textareas` and adds no token.

| Mode | Shown and typed as | Placeholder | Value in and out | Replaces |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `date` | `D MMM YYYY` | e.g. 17 Oct 2026 | `YYYY-MM-DD` | `type="date"` |
| `datetime` | `D MMM YYYY, HH:mm` | e.g. 17 Oct 2026, 14:05 | `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm`, local time | `type="datetime-local"` |
| `time` | `HH:mm`, 24-hour | e.g. 14:05 | `HH:mm` | `type="time"` |

- **Anatomy.** A persistent label above (C14); a text input with the family's field treatments (`--field-height`, `--border-interactive`, `--focus-ring`, and the hover, disabled, read-only and error states of `§ Inputs, selects and textareas`); a trailing icon button named "Choose date", "Choose date and time" or "Choose time" (the shared strings DS.42 to DS.44, C15); under the field, the zone caption "Local ({timeZone})" (DS.50) in the date-and-time and time modes, or the error. The placeholder is the example, never the label.
- **Typing.** The text is read on blur against the fixed English month table: "17 Oct 2026", "17 oct 2026", "17 October 2026" and "2026-10-17" are accepted and rewritten to the shown format. Text that cannot be read stays as typed and states the format (DS.45 to DS.47); a value outside `min` or `max` says so (DS.48, DS.49). An empty field stays empty; an optional one says "(optional)" in its label.
- **Calendar popover.** The range picker's popover and the DateTimeField's popover are one month grid, so the three labels above (header, day, weekday) serve both. It is the range picker's own grid (`DateTimeRangePicker.tsx`) on `--surface` with `--shadow-1`, a 1 px `--border`, `--type-popup-title` 14/20 and `--type-popup-supporting` 12/16; the chosen day filled `--accent-solid` with `--accent-on-solid` (as `DateTimeRangePicker.tsx:401` already does); days outside `min` and `max` `aria-disabled`. The date-and-time mode puts an `HH:mm` field under the grid. Month and weekday names come from the same fixed English table.
- **Keyboard.** Enter or Down in the field, or Enter on the button, opens the popover with focus on the chosen day (today when there is none); arrows move by day, Up and Down by week, PageUp and PageDown by month, Home and End to the week's edges, Enter chooses and closes, Escape closes and returns focus to the button. Typing never needs the popover.
- **Text-only variant.** Inside a popover that already has a month grid (the analytics range picker) the control is the typed field alone: two `date` and two `time` fields on that popover, with no second popover.
- **Time zone.** The value is local time and the caption names the zone. Three fields feed UTC into a local input today (`AddUserFeature.tsx:111`, `AddClassFeature.tsx:145` and `ListAllFeatures.tsx:63` use `toISOString().slice(0, -1)`); the implementation passes local time (`format(date, "yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm")`) so that the caption is true.

**The native controls to replace** (generated from the `format` rows that carry a date mode, so the list cannot drift from the index; the implementation plan's hand-off):

| Page | Anchor | Native input | Becomes |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-09 `/consent` | src/pages/Consent.tsx:65 | `type="date"` | DateTimeField (date): 17 Oct 2026 |
| A-03 `/admin/analytics` | src/components/DateTimeRangePicker/DateTimeRangePicker.tsx:437 | `type="date"` | DateTimeField (date): 17 Oct 2026 |
| A-03 `/admin/analytics` | src/components/DateTimeRangePicker/DateTimeRangePicker.tsx:473 | `type="date"` | DateTimeField (date): 17 Oct 2026 |
| A-03 `/admin/analytics` | src/components/DateTimeRangePicker/DateTimeRangePicker.tsx:450 | `type="time"` | DateTimeField (time): 14:05 |

12 controls in 8 files. A control that two pages show (the class access form, `AddClassFeature.tsx:144`, on A-06 and A-15) is listed under each page and counted once.

### Terminology

One word per concept (Podmajersky's terminology agreement; Nielsen #4). The left column is the word the interface uses; the right is what it replaces.

| Say | Not | Where it showed |
|---|---|---|
| sign in, sign out, sign-in (the noun) | log in, login, logout | the button, the top bar's tooltip, the register link, the OAuth page |
| Kiwi | Kiwi Platform | three email footers, the admin notification |
| allowlist | whitelist | the app registry's tab and dialogs, the users dialog, the CSV file name |
| class; cover image | course, course space; course image | the class form and the card |
| instructor | professor | form fields, role chips, the recipient filter |
| student for a role, user for a mixed group, roster for a class's people | "enrolled users", "students" for everyone | Enroll students, the email dialog |
| chat (Kiwi chat) | session (for the tutor's conversation), conversation | the panel's session bar and history |
| session, in analytics only (one run of a user's activity) | session for a chat; "visit" | "Sessions", "Average session" and "Session ID" on the analytics pages are kept: they count activity, not chats |
| sign-in (the signed-in period: "Your sign-in has expired") | session, for signing in | the chat's expiry error and its refresh line (SR-6.13, SR-6.14), the sign-in page (S-05.06); `check-copy.mjs` (D8) allows "session" in a new string only on the analytics page |
| instructions (what staff write for Kiwi) | system prompt, prompts | the class form (A-13) and the experiment form (A-20): the tutor's instructions only |
| prompt (a registered app's admin-approved prompt), and its text, system prompt | instructions, for this object | the app registry (A-08, A-12, A-23). It keeps its own words: the SDK's (`kiwi-app-sdk/README.md:111`, `contextualChat` "runs an admin-approved prompt"), the MCP tools' (`kiwi_app_list_prompts`) and the API's `systemPrompt` field, which the apps' developers use |
| model | engine | the experiment form, the analytics legend |
| group (the students) and variant (the chat setup) | variant groups | the experiments pages |
| feature flag, flag | assignment | the two feature-flag pages |
| API token (a credential); usage limit (what a student runs out of) | token, for both; "out of tokens" | webhooks; the chat's quota error |
| MCP server (a registered tool provider), always in full on the MCP pages; Kiwi's server (the backend) in a cause line there | server, for either: "this server", "Create server", "global servers" | the MCP pages (A-07, A-22, S-08): every label, button, tooltip, message and cause line (`check-copy.mjs` H18). Elsewhere "the server" in a cause line is Kiwi's backend (§ Errors) |
| identifier | slug | the app registry |
| setup | config | the experiments pages |
| answer | response | the chat toolbar |
| choose (an option, a file, a date); select (a control a sentence points to: "Select Retry") | pick | the DateTimeField's range errors (DS.48, DS.49), the Replace dialog (A-14.24), the variant picker (SR-6.12) |
| assistant (the experiment assistant, the panel that drafts an experiment's setup; "the assistant" once it is named), and the role "Teaching assistant" | AI assistant, design assistant, chat assistant; assistant for the tutor, who is Kiwi (C8) | the experiments pages (A-19, A-20), the panel (SR-7), the role list (A-08.71) |
| data (of a webhook event) | payload | the approvals table's column and details heading, the preview message, the secret's tooltip |
| deactivate, for a webhook (it stops receiving events and can be turned on again) | delete, remove, for the webhook | the webhook dialog's title, button, tooltip and toast and its failed-action line (A-09.08 to A-09.10, A-09.42, A-09.59, A-09.73); the API token's own control is a real delete and keeps the word |
| scratchpad (the tab); Code (its first file tab) | code space, IDE | the student code page |

**Model names.** One lookup turns an engine id into the name the interface shows, so the chat's select, the analytics legend and the experiment chips cannot disagree.

| Engine id | Shown as | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `openai` | ChatGPT | the chat's model select (`ChatCore.tsx:1494`), the analytics legend (`StandaloneUserEngine.tsx:66`), the variant chips (A-19, A-20, A-21), the compare mode's picker and column chips (`ChatCore.tsx:1275`, `:1333`, `ExperimentCompare.tsx:91`), the answer dialog (`ChatDetailDialog.tsx:335`), the experiment form's variant select (A-20: its label becomes "Model" and its options show these names, with the id as the value), the assistant's example text |
| `qwen` | Qwen | the same places |
| `unknown` | Unknown | the analytics legend |
| any other id | the id as written | never hidden |

A chip does not lowercase its label (`ExperimentList.tsx:192` sets `textTransform: lowercase`, which would turn ChatGPT into chatgpt); a raw id such as `openai` is never drawn.

A sentence may point to a control or page by its label as the screen spells it ("Select Retry", "Create one on the Experiments page."); the list of such names the lint accepts is `UI_REFS` in `.design-foundations/copy/copy-lib.mjs`.

**Labels for ids and codes.** The interface never draws an enum value, a key or an id as the program names it ("QwenModel", "pending", a class's UUID). A lookup turns each into the word the reader knows, one table per kind, so a select, a table cell, a chip, a tooltip and a sentence cannot disagree; a value a table does not list is drawn as written, never hidden. The tables are generated from `.design-foundations/copy/identifiers.json`, which is also what the checker reads: every index row that carries a lookup lists the values its place can show, and `check-copy.mjs` (H11) reads the source with the TypeScript compiler and fails when a place draws an enum value, a key, an id or a code with no lookup row and no exemption below.

<!-- copy:lookups:start -->
**Feature flags.** The flag select, the assignments tables and the edit-expiration sentence on A-06 and A-15 draw a feature by its label. What each flag does is read from the backend, not guessed.

| Value | Shown as | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `QwenModel` | Qwen model | The flag for the Qwen model; an experiment grants it to its participants (`experiment/experiment.service.ts:609`). |
| `TokenLimit` | No usage limit | Removes the usage limit for the class or user. The flag exempts; it does not set a limit, so "Usage limit" would say the opposite. |
| `RLanguageClass` | R in the scratchpad | Shows R beside Python in the scratchpad (`ClassIDE.tsx:134`); a class flag applies to that class, a user flag to every class. |

Rows: `A-06.04`, `A-06.12`, `A-06.13`, `A-15.04`, `A-15.10`, `A-06.14`.

Rows: `A-09.04`, `A-09.87`.

**Ids shown by name.** A UUID is never drawn as text. A select, a chip, a table cell or a sentence that names a class, a user or a webhook draws its name, read as the code already reads it elsewhere; while the name is loading the place waits (a skeleton, never the id), and when it cannot be read the fallback below stands in.

| An id of | Shown as | When it cannot be read | Rows |
|---|---|---|---|
| class | the class's name (`classControllerGetById(...).className`, the read `AdminLayout.tsx:340` already makes) | "Untitled class" | `A-06.05`, `A-06.14`, `A-06.15`, `A-11.51`, `A-15.02`, `A-15.03`, `A-15.11`, `A-16.30`, `A-16.50` |
| user | the user's email address (the Users page's own column) | "Unknown user" | `A-06.14`, `A-06.16` |
| webhook | its address, the URL the admin entered | "this webhook" | `A-09.81` |

**Identifiers drawn as they are.** A few places draw an id or a code on purpose. Each is listed with its reason; `check-copy.mjs` (H11) fails on any other.

### Errors

An error says what happened, why, and what to do (Yifrah; Nielsen #9). It never blames the reader, never ends without a way out, never uses "Failed to" or "Oops", and says what is safe when something is (Yifrah).

**Anatomy.** Three parts, each a short sentence: **what happened** (specific, in the reader's nouns, "Couldn't …"), **why** (a cause from the closed set below, or the rule that was broken), **what to do** (one imperative that names the control or the person). A fourth sentence, between the why and the what to do, says what is safe where that is true (`What is safe`, below). A message that sits beside a Retry button says "Select Retry" or "Try again". An input error opens with a sentence that states what is wrong, the rule applied to this field ("The email has no subject.", "That isn't a date Kiwi can read."), and then gives the correction as an imperative ("Add one, then send."). It never opens with the correction: "Enter a date like 17 Oct 2026." says what to do and not what is wrong with what was typed. That first sentence is the what-happened part, so the message needs no "Couldn't …" sentence of its own, and it takes a cause sentence between the two only where the statement does not already give the cause. The field's error state (the field is marked and named, with the cross icon and the message tied to it by `aria-describedby`, `§ Components`) carries where the error is.

**The cause lines.** The interface can know little, so the set is closed and the generic case is honest. When the UI does not know why, the cause is "The server didn't respond as expected": true whether the request was refused, dropped or failed, and never a guess presented as a fact. It is never used where the page holds the server's own message: that case quotes it (`Where a message comes from`, below). A `{status}` after a server error is a support reference, not the message.

| Code | When | Cause line | Default fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| `net` | No response arrived (offline, timeout) | "Kiwi couldn't reach the server." | "Check your connection, then try again." |
| `server` | A 5xx or an unexpected response | "The server returned an error." | "Try again in a moment." |
| `unknown` | A generic catch: the UI has no status and no message | "The server didn't respond as expected." | "Try again." |
| `expired` | The sign-in has really ended | "Your sign-in has expired." | "Sign in again." |
| `denied` | A refused permission | "Your account doesn't have access to {this}." or the page's own line | "Ask {an administrator, your instructor} for access." |
| `input` | A rule the reader broke, or a value that is missing | The rule: "It must start with http:// or https://." | The correction: "Correct it and try again." |
| `conflict` | A 409 | What already exists: "Some are already on the roster." | The way round: "Remove them from the list and try again." |
| `quota` | A 429 | "You've reached your usage limit." | "Ask your instructor or an administrator about it." |
| `missing` | A 404, or a link without its target | "The link has no class." | "Choose a class from the class switcher." |
| `partial` | Some of a batch succeeded, or a loop stopped partway | "The server didn't respond as expected for the other {m}." | "Refresh the list and try those again." |

**On the MCP pages the server has a name.** "The server" in a cause line is Kiwi's own backend, but on the MCP pages (A-07, A-22, S-08) a reader would take it for the MCP server. There the same four lines name Kiwi's server, and a line about the MCP server itself names it:

| Code | On the MCP pages | Everywhere else |
|---|---|---|
| `net` | "Kiwi couldn't reach its server." | "Kiwi couldn't reach the server." |
| `server` | "Kiwi's server returned an error." | "The server returned an error." |
| `unknown` | "Kiwi's server didn't respond as expected." | "The server didn't respond as expected." |
| `partial` | "Kiwi's server didn't respond as expected for the other {m}." | "The server didn't respond as expected for the other {m}." |

Where the MCP server is the party that failed (a connection test with no message, a client registration, an OAuth discovery), the row says so: "The MCP server didn't respond as expected." The record itself is "MCP server" in full on these pages, in every label, button, tooltip and message, never "server" alone (the one exception is the transport's name, "Server-sent events"); `check-copy.mjs` (H18) reads every string on them.

**Kiwi once.** A cause line that opens with "Kiwi" ("Kiwi couldn't reach the server.") opens with "It" after a sentence that already names Kiwi: "Kiwi couldn't finish answering. It couldn't reach the server. Try again." The name is said once (`check-copy.mjs` K5 fails on a string with two sentences that open with it).

**What is safe.** A failed save, create, update, send, change, add, upload, assign, enroll or register (a failed write) whose page leaves the form as the person filled it says so in one closed sentence, "Your changes are still in the form.", placed between the cause and the next step, so nobody retypes what is still there. It is written only where the page really keeps the input: the dialog closes, or the fields clear, only after the call returns, and the catch block only sets the message. Of the 72 failed-write rows, 38 carry it and each cites the line that keeps the input; 34 leave it out and each says why, in the row's note: there is no form (an inline switch or select that the page puts back, a button on a saved item, a region drawn on a page), the page clears what was typed (the allowlist box after a run, a comment the annotation page drops, an announcement whose handler swallows the error), a batch leaves the box holding addresses that already worked, or the fix says to refresh the list, which drops the form. A failed write with neither a `safe` nor a `nosafe` line fails `check-copy.mjs` (D11), and so does a catch block that clears the form while its row says the input is kept. Where a page clears a draft on failure, the sentence waits for a flow change (`§ Hand-offs`).

**A batch is a partial result.** A function that sends requests one after another (bulk approve, bulk reject, add addresses to the allowlist, create accounts, upload files, assign servers, enable apps) can stop partway, and what it reports is then a partial result: **what** went through (`Approved {n} of {total: count event}.`), **why** the rest did not (the cause above, scoped to the rest: "The server didn't respond as expected for the other {m}."; where the page lists a reason for each item, "The server returned an error for the other {m}." and the fix points at the list; where a loop has no count, "…, and some may already have changed"), and **what to do** ("Refresh the list and try those again."). A count of failures is never the why (`check-copy.mjs` D1), and every such function has a row (H14). A loop that only reads and changes nothing is listed with its reason.

Wording that depends on the cause is written for both ends, and the specific one ships with the generic one: the implementation plan's status mapping and the fallback arrive together, so the generic line is only what a failure with no matching status gets (S-05 has all four: the generic S-05.03, the refused sign-in S-05.04, the unreachable server S-05.05 and a server error S-05.16; S-05.03 never ships alone, because on its own it would tell a wrong password that the server did not respond as expected). **The sign-in wording depends on defect B2.** "Your sign-in has expired" (S-05.06) is true only once a refused permission no longer signs the user out. That is fixed on the branch `fix/access-control`: a signed-in user who may not do something gets a 403, and the web client keeps the sign-in on a 403, with no sign-out and no alert. S-05.06 ships with or after that merge; until then a refused permission also lands on that text. A refused permission uses the `denied` cause or the no-permission strings (DS.29, DS.30), never the sign-in wording; after the merge the native 403 alert (DS.33) is gone and the 404 alert (DS.34) stays. **The enrolment line depends on the same merge.** S-01.07 ("Your instructor or an administrator adds you to a class.") names the instructor because on `fix/access-control` the class menu shows Enroll students to a class's assigned instructors (LC-7); it ships with or after that merge, like S-05.06, and until then only an administrator's menu offers it.

**Raw backend text (C10).** It never stands alone. Kiwi's own sentence comes first; the server's words follow in straight quotes ("The server said: \"{message}\"."), wrapped (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`) and clamped to three lines. The clamped element is focusable (`tabindex="0"`) and its accessible description is the full text, so a keyboard user and a screen reader reach what the clamp hides, and the full text shows on hover and on focus, as a truncated table cell's does (the truncated span is focusable there too). On a touch screen, where there is no hover, a visible text button after the message, "Show full message" (then "Hide full message": DS.51, DS.52), lifts the clamp as well, so the words the clamp hides are reachable on every device and with every input. Only a message the backend wrote for the person who reads it is quoted: a body that carries a stack, a query, a path or an internal name is the backend's defect, and the hand-off asks for user-safe messages. When the server gives no message the quoted part is left out.

**Where a message comes from.** A failed call reaches a page in three shapes, and each is handled once. (1) The generated client throws `ResponseError`, whose `message` is always "Response returned an error code" (`services/runtime.ts:140`), or `FetchError` ("The request failed and the interceptors did not return an alternative response"): the library's text, never shown (C16). Where today's code shows `error.message`, the page shows the row's own sentence; the index marks each such site (`dev`). (2) The server's own words, in a body the page reads (`await error.response.json()`, as `UserManagement.tsx` does; the generated client sets no `response.data`, so `error.response.data.message` is always undefined): the row quotes them after Kiwi's sentence, `The server said: "{message}"` (C10), and the generic row is the fallback when the body has none (`srv`). Only a message in the response body is quoted: a reason phrase ("Internal Server Error") is not the server's words, and belongs to (3). The app's own fetch helpers (`experimentApi.ts`, `classAnalyticsApi.ts`) throw an Error whose message is the body's `message` or `error` or, when the body has neither, the status's reason phrase; they must keep the two apart (the Error carries `status`, and its `body` holds the body's text only when there is some), so that a page quotes the body (2) or gives the status cause (3), never the phrase. A parser's message (`JSON.parse`) is developer text (C16) and is never quoted: the row names where the problem is in plain words when the parser gives a position ("Check the text near character {n}.", read from "at position {n}") and gives a generic hint when it does not ("Look through the text for one that is missing or extra."), after a why that states the rule JSON follows ("JSON needs each comma, quote and bracket in place, with none extra.") (`dev`). (3) A status line ("HTTP 500: Internal Server Error") or a reason phrase (`statusText`): a status code stands in for words (C9), so each handler keeps its own sentence and the cause line is "The server returned an error ({status}).", placed before the action sentence in place of the generic cause, never added after "Try again." (`http`). One rule for both: a status line or a reason phrase always takes this line, and quoting (2) applies to a message in a response body only. `check-copy.mjs` (H5) lists every site in the source that shows such a message and fails when one has no row.

**Where this section supersedes the Companion's hand-off.** `§ Companion › Hand-offs` tells the implementation plan to class the chat's old paths as retryable, quota or permission, or backend text, and puts `No token` and `Token expired` under retryable. That line is locked text, so this section supersedes it for those two paths, in the same way Phase 6 replaces the interim chart stand-ins (`§ Hand-offs` of Phase 3). **"No token"** (`ChatCore.tsx:766`, `useChatLane.ts:126`: there is no sign-in to send the question with) is the `expired` cause of SR-6.13, "Couldn't send your question. Your sign-in has expired. Your question isn't saved. Copy it, then reload the page to sign in again.", and it never offers Retry, because trying again with no sign-in cannot work. What the student sees, from the code: the question leaves the composer when the send starts and stays in the thread (`ChatCore.tsx:1041`); the no-token branch shows this sentence in a toast and returns before anything is sent (`ChatCore.tsx:762-768`), so the question is never saved and the conversation a reload restores, read back from the server, does not have it (`ChatCore.tsx:253-269`). A reload reaches the sign-in page, because every path that drops the token drops the stored user too (`middleware.ts:27-28`, `:47`, `:66-67`, `:82-83`) and the route guard sends a visit with no user to it (`ProtectedRoute.tsx:51`); the sentence names that reload, and adds no control, which would be a flow change. **"Token expired"** (SR-6.14, `ChatCore.tsx:797`) is not an error at all: it is the status while the app renews the sign-in by itself ("Refreshing your sign-in…", then "Retrying your question…", SR-6.15), and the student does nothing. When the renewal fails, the app signs the student out and opens the sign-in page (`ChatCore.tsx:798-806`, `useChatLane.ts:152-160`); that page shows its expiry message (S-05.06) only for `?timeout=true` (`Login.tsx:101-105`) and these paths send `?sessionExpired=true`, so today it shows nothing, and the implementation plan makes it show S-05.06 for both (`§ Hand-offs`). The chat draws no error row for a failed renewal. Every other path keeps the Phase 4 mapping. `check-copy.mjs` (K4) fails if this supersession is missing or if a row with the `expired` cause offers Retry.

### Confirmations, empty states, progress and fields

- **Confirmation (C11).** Title: "{Verb} {object}?" (never "Are you sure?"). Body: what happens to it, then whether it can be undone. "Consequence first" means the first sentence of the body: the title only names the action and its object. Buttons: "Cancel" and "{Verb} {object}" ("Deactivate webhook", "Unassign MCP server", "Remove entry", "Disable app"). A reversible removal gets an Undo toast and no dialog (Nielsen #3).
- **Empty state (C12).** First use: "No {things} yet" and one sentence with the first step, and one primary button ("Create first class") for a role that can act. No action for this role: "{Who acts} adds {things}. They will appear here." and no button. No results: "No {things} match \"{query}\"" and "Check the spelling, or search for another name." A failed load is the frame's error form, "Couldn't load {things}", with its cause line and Retry. The heading and any one-clause line that reports an absence ("No classes assigned to you yet") take no full stop; the sentence that explains or instructs keeps its own (C5). The sidebar's list has no body line, so the instructor's empty state there is one line of two sentences, the heading's words and then who acts ("No classes assigned to you yet. An administrator assigns them."), each with its full stop; the page draws the same heading and a longer body (A-01.17, A-01.14).
- **Progress (C13).** "Loading {things}…", "Saving…", "Sending…"; a long job states a duration or a percentage only when it is measured, and otherwise says what is happening and nothing more ("Analyzing student questions…"). The walk state's steps have their own table (`§ Step labels`).
- **Fields (C14).** The label is "Class name"; the helper adds ("Shown in the subject and the email header."); the placeholder is an example ("e.g. The lab moves to room 301 on Friday"); optional fields end "(optional)".

### Step labels

The walk state (`§ Companion › The nine states`) has words for every moment of a turn, and they are written once, here. **The wait.** Before the first step or token the loader says "Thinking…", the word followed by its cycling dots (SR-6.69), which are its ellipsis and rest as the ellipsis character when motion is reduced (Phase 3's text dots): the user's own wording (G9) and the one concession to the candour of `§ Voice`. **The steps.** Once a step runs, its label names the step: the chain's header reads "Working…" while the turn runs (and "N steps" when it ends), and each step has a label of one of three kinds, so the student can tell what Kiwi is doing and whether it touches the page. A step that Kiwi cannot name reads "Working…", the header's word, with the ellipsis (C13): it says that Kiwi is working and claims no process the product cannot show.

| Moment or kind | What Kiwi does | The label | The labels |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Wait** | Nothing to show yet: the first step or token has not arrived | "Thinking…" with the cycling dots | "Thinking…" (SR-6.69): the word and its dots, in the loader |
| **Reading** | Looks at something and changes nothing on the page | Starts with Checking, Looking up, Reading or Searching | "Checking the page list" (SR-6.41), "Looking up your classes" (SR-6.42), "Reading this page" (SR-6.45), "Reading class analytics" (kept), "Searching your class materials" (SR-6.48) |
| **Acting** | Operates the page on the student's behalf | Names what Kiwi does and ends "for you" | "Opening a page for you" (SR-6.43), "Opening class materials for you" (SR-6.44), "Clicking for you" (SR-6.46), "Filling in a field for you" (SR-6.47) |
| **Tool** | Runs one of its own tools, away from the page | "Running ⟨what⟩", "Using ⟨tool⟩…" on the tool pill, or the tool's own name; never "for you" | "Running code" (kept); "Using {displayName}…" (SR-6.53), the name being "Materials search" (SR-6.55), "Code interpreter" (SR-6.56) or the tool's own, "Search papers" (SR-6.57); in the chain the name alone, "Search papers" (SR-6.68) |
| **No name** | Cannot name the step | "Working…" | "Working…" as the label of a step with no name (SR-6.49), and "Working…" (kept) as the chain's header while the turn runs |

Four notes. The Phase 4 draft grouped running code with the acting steps ("Doing ⟨action⟩…"); it does not operate the page, so "for you" would say what is not true, and it is a tool step. The shorthand "Running ⟨tool⟩…" in `§ Companion › Collisions` (the brief's own words) names this kind. The same tool can show in the chain and on the pill: the chain's label is the step, and the pill appears only where an answer's text begins with "Using …" (`ChatMessages.tsx:140-159`). **Tests.** `AgentStepChain.test.tsx:80` asks for the header with `getByText("Working…")` and passes while every step is named; a test that draws a step with no name finds two, so it reads the header by its button (the revision index lists the line on SR-6.49). **One step, two kinds.** "Searching your class materials" (SR-6.48) is a tool's step and also a look at something that changes nothing, so it fits **Reading** and **Tool**. Reading wins, because the kind answers the student's question of whether Kiwi is touching the page or the work, and a search only looks, as reading a page does; **Tool** is what names the tool itself, so the same search is "Materials search" (SR-6.55) on the pill and where the chain shows the name alone. The sentence belongs to the built-in materials search and to no other tool: the step chain matches `/rag|search/i` today (`AgentStepChain.tsx:125`), which any tool with the word search in its name also matches, so the implementation matches the built-in tool's exact name (`§ Hand-offs`).

### Shared strings

The final wording of every recurring string the design system quoted as a placeholder, with the placeholder it supersedes (two rows, DS.33 and DS.34, supersede the strings the code shows today, which no Phase 3 table quotes). Phase 3's component tables are locked text, so this table, generated from the same data as the revision index (route `DS`), is the one to implement. A `{slot}` is filled at run time; "kept" means the placeholder was already right.

### Companion text

The nine text twins in `§ Companion › The nine states` are final: the five that carried draft wording are revised in place (the revision index's `CP` rows) and the other four ("none" for `peek`, `retract` and `idle`, and the completion announcement for `hop`) were already right. The completion string itself is new copy, since nothing in the source announces completion today, so it and "Feedback saved." have their own `CP` rows.

- **The three live-region strings:** completion, "Kiwi finished answering." (polite, once, then focus moves to the composer); feedback, "Feedback saved." (polite, once); offline, "You're offline. Waiting for your connection." (the composer's status line). The offline line states a condition and promises nothing: the student chat does re-send after a transport error (`@microsoft/fetch-event-source` retries when `onerror` returns, `ChatCore.tsx:968-974`), but the compare lanes stop (`useChatLane.ts:241`), so no retry is claimed. Principle 4 reads "every state that conveys information has a text twin": `peek`, `retract` and `idle` convey nothing and read "none".
- **The error row:** retryable ("Kiwi couldn't finish answering." + a cause line + "Try again." beside Retry), quota, permission, expired ("Couldn't send your question. Your sign-in has expired. Your question isn't saved. Copy it, then reload the page to sign in again.", no Retry: the supersession in `§ Errors`), and backend text (quoted and wrapped), as the table spells them.
- **The `walk` twin:** "Thinking…" with the cycling dots until the first step starts (the loader, SR-6.69), then the chain's header "Working…" and one label per step. A **reading** step's label starts with Checking, Looking up, Reading or Searching and changes nothing; an **acting** step's label names what Kiwi does and ends "for you", because it operates the page on the student's behalf; a **tool** step, which runs one of Kiwi's own tools away from the page, reads "Running code" or the tool's own name (or "Using ⟨tool⟩…" on the tool pill) and never ends "for you"; a step with no name reads "Working…". `§ Step labels` lists every label with its kind, so the student can tell the kinds apart.
- **Pending decision A-7 (the user's).** Whether a dislike gets a brief neutral mascot acknowledgement, as a like gets a wave, is not decided here. The like and the dislike text reads correctly either way: the live-region string is "Feedback saved." for both thumbs, the pressed thumb keeps its name, "Thumbs up" or "Thumbs down", with `aria-pressed` saying it is pressed, and neither string mentions a clip. Whichever way the decision goes changes no word in this section.

### Email templates

The copy ships once, in both places: the browser preview (`TemplatedEmailForm.tsx`, and the prefilled message of `EmailSelectedUsersDialog.tsx`) and the backend templates that recipients receive (`kiwi/src/email/email.templates.ts`), so the preview and the mail agree (SF-20). **One source per string:** the table below is that source, and the revision index's `EM` rows hold each changed string with both anchors. The welcome heading applies one institution-name rule in both places: `brandLabel` is "{institutionName} Kiwi" when an institution name is configured and "Kiwi" when it is not (the rule of `BRAND_LABEL` in `src/config/branding.ts`, so an unbranded deployment never reads "Kiwi Kiwi"; the backend applies it to its `INSTITUTION_NAME`). The enrollment body is one text: the dialog's prefilled message for an active user is the enrollment email's first sentence and its "already in your classes" line, word for word. Colours are Phase 3's. Every footer reads "Sent by Kiwi." because the old "Please do not reply to this email" is not reliably true: the sender sets a Reply-To address (`kiwi/src/config/configuration.ts`). The support line appears only where a support address is configured (C17). The sender's display names default to "Kiwi" and "Kiwi support" (`configuration.ts:13` and `:17`), not "Kiwi Platform" and "Kiwi Platform Support"; `docs/email.md` quotes the old defaults and the old notification subject (lines 28, 29 and 91).

| Template | Subject | Header and sub-header | Body | Footer |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Class announcement | `Class announcement: {{className}}` | "Class announcement" / the class name | "From: {professorName}" when set; then the message as typed | "Sent by Kiwi." |
| Important notification | `Important notification from Kiwi` | "Kiwi" / "Important notification" | The title as a heading; the highlighted box, which opens with the label "Notice" (EM.29, the word Phase 3's mapping promises) and then holds the message | "Sent by Kiwi." |
| Welcome | `You can now sign in to Kiwi` | "Welcome to {brandLabel}" | "Hello," · "You can now sign in to Kiwi with the email address this message was sent to." · the link "Open Kiwi" · when a guide exists, "New to Kiwi? Read the guide to get started." · when a support address exists, "Need help? Contact {SUPPORT_EMAIL}." (no "Happy Kiwi-ing!", no sign-off) | "Sent by Kiwi." |
| Class enrollment | `You've been added to {{className}} on Kiwi` | "Welcome to Kiwi" / the class name | "You've been added to {className}{ ({professorName})} on Kiwi." (the part in the second braces is left out when the name is not set) · the sign-in line, which says "The class appears in your classes when you sign in." for a Google account, "The class will appear in your classes." once the account is set up, or "It's already in your classes at {url}." · "Need help? Contact {SUPPORT_EMAIL}." when set. The dialog's prefilled message for an active user is the first sentence without that part and "It's already in your classes at {url}.", with the support line when set | "Sent by Kiwi." |

The plain-text versions carry the same words; the notification's plain-text header is "Kiwi: important notification" (it was typed in capitals).

### Scope

In: every user-facing string under `kiwi-ui/src` (outside generated `src/services/`), the native `alert()`, `confirm()` and `prompt()` text passed in (their dialogs are the browser's), and the outgoing email templates. Out, with the reason: the tutor's generated answers and the class instructions (the model's and the instructor's, plan OUT); embedded Kiwi App content (G5); model-facing strings (`src/agent/*` tool descriptions and tool-result messages) and the replies the app bridge sends to an embedded app; the widget host (`src/apps/host/*`), which is not mounted; orphaned files that no route imports (SF-09: `Analytics/UserLogin`, `SessionDaily`, `PDFType`, `RecitationDetail/`, `Register/Register.tsx`, `ThumbnailViewer/`, `Header/`, `ClassManagement.tsx`, `utils/documentPipeline.ts`, `utils/validatePythonFile.ts`); `console.*` output; the student's own program output (a Python traceback, R console text), and an R result string that nothing draws (`WebR.tsx:141`: `IDE.tsx:191` takes `stdoutLines`, which is always an array, so the `results` branch at `:194` never runs); and translation (the UI stays English). Chart units, axis names and chart text summaries are Phase 6.

| Rule coverage | 18 rules, 9 § Errors items, 10 § Dates rows, 5 step kinds and 24 terminology rows each have a lint over the strings with a planted defect that proves it, or a statement of what no lint can see with the rows read by hand (406 rows in 8 samples); the proof run (`prove-checks.mjs`) plants 189 strings into the lints (45 controls that must stay quiet) and 227 defects into a throwaway copy of the data, the guide and the source, and every one is caught by the check it names (2 further change to a copy is a control that must leave its check quiet); the table is in `§ Rule coverage` | R1 |

### Rule coverage

<!-- copy:coverage:start -->
Read from `.design-foundations/copy/rule-coverage.json` and `generated/proof-summary.json` at assembly time, never typed. A **lint** is a check that runs over strings (`over`: **new** every New string, **kept** every kept string and every source string no row touches, **source** every string in the source, **examples** the guide's own examples, **code** the source code the checks read, **data** the revision data, **guide** this text); a **planted defect** is a string or an edit that breaks the rule, put into a throwaway copy by `prove-checks.mjs`, and the lint is proved only when the run reports it caught (189 unit proofs, 45 of them controls that must stay quiet, and 229 end-to-end proofs, 2 of them a control that must stay quiet, all behaving as planted). What no lint can see is checked by hand: the rows read, and the result.

| Rule | Lints that run over the strings | Planted defects | Checked by hand |
|---|---|---|---|
| `C1` Case | B1 (new), H17 (kept), H1 (source), H7 (code); the audit's own: C1-A, C1-B | 10 unit, 7 end to end | nothing a lint cannot see |
| `C2` Buttons | B1 (new), H12 (code), H8 (data); the audit's own: C2-A | 8 unit, 4 end to end | 11 rows read: 11 of 11 need the extra words: 3 name a provider (Sign in with Google, Sign up with Google), 2 are a count and its noun (Approve 3 events), 1 is the person's own voice (I've copied the secret), 1 is two actions in one button (Sign in and allow access), 4 carry a qualifier that separates the button from its neighbour (Ask Kiwi to check, Go to your classes, Create first MCP server, Assign existing MCP servers); none can lose a word without losing its object |
| `C3` Dates and times | B1 (new), H17 (kept), H1 (source), H2 (code), H20 (guide); the audit's own: C3-A | 12 unit, 6 end to end | nothing a lint cannot see |
