### Hand-offs

- **Phase 6 (Data surfaces):** series and category names take sentence case and the vocabulary above ("Chat action", "Model choice"); counts read `1,234`; dates read C3; each chart's text summary and each table's units follow the Steady and Exact voice and are appended to `## Revision index` (`P6-` ids) before Phase 7 reads it. The chart axis helper draws four ticks and tooltips with a fixed `en-US` locale, which C3 does not allow, all in `chartXAxis.ts`: the date tick (`chartXAxis.ts:50`, `Oct 7`), the midnight tick (`chartXAxis.ts:80`, the same), the time tick (`chartXAxis.ts:85`, `3pm`, a 12-hour clock) and the tooltip (`chartXAxis.ts:91`, `Oct 7, 3:30 PM`). Phase 6 gives each its C3 form (day first, the 24-hour `HH:mm`, the fixed month table; a tick may leave the year out where the axis names it, and its `P6-` row says so), and the date guard has no `en-US` display call left to allow.
- **Phase 7 (Final mocks):** every string in the 16 renders is the as-is string in its page spec or its revised form in the index (DW-7.7), the shared strings and the Companion twins; dates use C3; no placeholder text.
- **Implementation plan (the components and logic the words depend on):** build `DateTimeField` (`§ Dates, times and numbers › The date control`) and replace the 12 native controls it lists; read the response body's `message` where a row quotes the server, drop `error.message` where a row says the library's text is never shown, and replace each `HTTP {status}: {statusText}` line with the cause line, placed before the action sentence in place of the generic cause (`§ Errors › Where a message comes from`); route the range label and the calendar's header, day and weekday names through the fixed table (`§ Dates, times and numbers`); give each `{SUPPORT_EMAIL}` sentence its two variants (a row with the address, a row without); ship S-05.06 and S-01.07 with or after the `fix/access-control` merge and the no-permission strings with the B4 fix; default the sender names to "Kiwi" and "Kiwi support" and fix `docs/email.md` lines 28, 29 and 91; one `engineLabel` lookup (`§ Terminology › Model names`); the Tests column lists every string, regular-expression and text reference as `file:line`.
- **Implementation plan (ids and codes):** add one `featureLabel`, `roleLabel`, `statusLabel` and `placementLabel` lookup per table in `§ Terminology › Labels for ids and codes` (the model-name lookup is the pattern: an unlisted value is drawn as written); resolve an id to its name before it is drawn (a class by `classControllerGetById(...).className`, a user by email, a webhook by its URL, with the skeleton while it loads and the fallback when it cannot be read), in the flag selects, the assignments tables (the DataGrid's Target column needs a `renderCell`), the edit-expiration sentence and the registration results; draw no UUID as text, and keep an id only where a column or row is labelled as one; tell each button its object (the one-word buttons H12 lists) and give the three loops that send requests in sequence (`ClassMCPSettings.tsx:347`, `ClassKiwiAppsSettings.tsx:180`, `Materials.tsx:213`) a partial-result message.
- **Implementation plan (confirmations):** twelve places ask the person to confirm (`.design-foundations/copy/confirmations.json`, found by the `H16` check): the approve and reject dialogs, the delete-webhook, regenerate-token, remove-allowlist-entry, disable-app and Materials dialogs (replace, delete material, delete file), two through the shared `DeleteConfirmDialog` and two native `confirm()` calls. Each has a question for a title, a body that gives the consequence, then permanence or the way back, and a button that repeats the verb and the object. Give the shared dialog `confirmLabel` and `busyLabel` props: it draws "Delete" and "Deleting…" whatever the dialog does, so the unassign dialog's "Unassign MCP server" and "Unassigning…" (A-22.15, A-22.49) cannot be met without them. The delete dialog's body is three sentences in order (A-07.13, then A-07.14 when the server is assigned, then A-07.85). The two native `confirm()` calls pass the index's New text as the string the browser shows (A-08.40, A-09.36); only the browser's own OK and Cancel buttons stay until a flow change turns them into dialogs, because C2 cannot reach them.
- **Implementation plan (failed files):** a file the server could not process shows its message under its name in the slot (A-14.29, A-14.30), in error colour, with the same words as the status chip's tooltip, so keyboard and touch users can read it (a tooltip alone is hover only); the slot's status chip and the material row's chip read "Not processed" (A-14.12, A-14.28), and the filter option keeps "Processing failed" (the kept entry in the A-14 `Copy (Phase 5)` bullet). The control to retry is the icon button named "Process this file again" (A-14.13), and the message names it.
- **Implementation plan (messages):** (1) the fetch helpers (`experimentApi.ts:120`, `classAnalyticsApi.ts:146`) set `error.status` and keep the reason phrase out of `error.message`, so a page can quote a body (`§ Errors` 2) or give the status cause (`§ Errors` 3), never the phrase; (2) clamped server text (C10) is a focusable element (`tabIndex={0}`) whose accessible description is the full text, so a keyboard user can reach what the clamp hides, not only a pointer user by hover; (3) the JSON position is read from `error.message` (`/position (\d+)/`) and the generic hint is used when there is none; (4) on the MCP pages the cause helper takes `variant: "mcp"`, so its lines name Kiwi's server (`§ Errors`); (5) the backend writes any message it returns in a response body for the person who reads it: a message that a page may quote (`§ Errors` 2) carries no stack, query, path, identifier or internal term, since a quoted body is the one place developer text could reach a screen (C10), and the endpoints the `srv` rows of the index quote are the ones to read; (6) on a touch screen (`(pointer: coarse)`), where hover does not exist, the clamped server text is followed by the text button "Show full message" and, once open, "Hide full message" (DS.51, DS.52), with `aria-expanded`; the full text stays the accessible description on every pointer.
- **Implementation plan (drafts kept after a failure):** "Your changes are still in the form." is true only while the page keeps the draft, and three failed-write rows leave it out because the page does not: the class email's announcement handler catches the error itself and returns (`ClassEmail.tsx:187-193`, `ClassEmail.tsx:236-242`), so `AnnouncementComposer.tsx:43-47` clears the message and the subject as if the send had worked (A-17.09, and A-17.19, which that composer's own catch would show if the handler threw); and the annotation page removes the pin and clears the typed comment when the save fails (`AnnotateDocument.tsx:1046-1054`, A-24.25). Rethrow the error in the first and keep the pin and the text in the second, then add the sentence to those three rows (give each a `safe` line in the revision data, and `check-copy.mjs` D11 reads the catch it cites). The allowlist box (`UserManagement.tsx:701`) is cleared after every run, failures included: keep the addresses that failed in the box, then A-11.34, A-11.42 and A-11.55 can say it too.
- **Implementation plan (the access model the words follow):** copy that says who can act follows the access model the user decided on 2026-10-16, which `fix/access-control` builds (`docs/logic-changes.md`, LC-2 and LC-7), and not today's menus and guards. System-level pages (System analytics, System email, Email audit, System flags, New class, Webhooks and their approvals, MCP servers, the App registry, Users) are admin-only, and so are Class flags and Class MCP and tools; on a class's pages its assigned instructors may use Enroll students, Class email and Class apps (an admin may use every page), and Materials and Class analytics when their permission allows; only an admin creates a class or assigns an instructor to one. So S-01.07 ("Your instructor or an administrator adds you to a class.") and S-04.03 ("Ask your instructor to turn it on.") name the instructor, and A-12.10 ("Ask an administrator or an instructor of this class to turn on {appName}.") names both: ship S-05.06 and S-01.07 with or after the merge, and draw A-12.09 ("Manage class apps") in the host frame for an admin and for an instructor assigned to the class (`AdminAppView.tsx:468-485` draws the button for admins alone), leaving A-12.10 for a reader who is neither. The strings that say who can act are listed in `access-claims.json` with the facts that back each one, and `check-copy.mjs` (D13) fails on one that is not.
- **Implementation plan (a claim the page cannot back):** the webhook's Delete is a soft delete: `webhooks/webhooks.service.ts:173-177` only sets it inactive, the Inactive tab lists it and the Edit dialog's Status turns it on again (`WebhookManagement.tsx:540-543`, `WebhookManagement.tsx:724-738`). Decided under C16 (nothing the page does not do), in one change with the index: the dialog's title is "Deactivate this webhook?" (A-09.08), its button is "Deactivate webhook" (A-09.10), the row's own control is "Deactivate webhook" too (A-09.73, the tooltip and the accessible name), the toast is "Webhook deactivated" (A-09.42), and A-09.09 says that the webhook stops receiving events and where to turn it on again. The API token's own control is a real delete and keeps its word. The row's icon is a trash can, which draws the word the copy no longer uses: Phase 6 and Phase 7 draw a power or pause glyph for it. A hard delete would be a product change, and the copy would change with it: the permanence sentence and the word delete come back in the same change. One other action is not quite what its word says: the material's Delete archives the material (`class/material/material.service.ts:73-84`), but no list shows an archived material and nothing restores it, so for the person it is a delete and the row keeps the word; if an archive list is added, the row is relabelled in one change (`check-copy.mjs` D12 reads every removing call in the source, and holds each to what its backend does).
- **Implementation plan (claims that wait for the code):** two strings that the guide's voice samples quote say something the code does not yet make true (`§ Rule coverage`, the guide's own examples), and each waits for one change. "Searching your class materials" (SR-6.48) belongs to the built-in materials search alone, but the step chain draws its label for every tool whose name matches `/rag|search/i` (`AgentStepChain.tsx:125`, looked up at `AgentStepChain.tsx:133-134`), so a tool such as `search_papers` from an MCP server would be told as a search of the class materials, and SR-6.68, which draws that tool by its own name, "Search papers", could never show: the lookup matches the built-in search by its exact tool name, and every other tool keeps its own name. "Feedback saved." (CP.13) is true only once the save has succeeded, but the thumb is marked at once and a failed save is only logged (`Feedback.tsx:24-32`, `useSaveInteraction.ts:51-58`): the page announces "Feedback saved." only after the save call has returned. A save that fails has no words yet, because the page has no failure path to draw them; the change that adds one writes them under `§ Errors`.
- **Implementation plan (the chat's error paths):** `§ Errors` supersedes the Phase 4 hand-off for two paths. `No token` (`ChatCore.tsx:766`, `useChatLane.ts:126`) is the expired cause (SR-6.13), drawn without Retry. The main chat's branch also leaves the answering state on: it sets the state (`ChatCore.tsx:759`), shows the sentence in a toast and returns (`ChatCore.tsx:764-768`), so the loader and the Stop button stay until the student presses Stop or reloads; a compare lane ends its loader with the same sentence (`useChatLane.ts:124-128`), so end the main chat's loader the same way, and the sentence becomes a row in the thread and not only a toast. `Token expired` (`ChatCore.tsx:797`) is the automatic renewal, drawn as the status "Refreshing your sign-in…" and then "Retrying your question…" (SR-6.14, SR-6.15), never as an error row. When the renewal fails the code sends the student to `/login?sessionExpired=true` (`ChatCore.tsx:803`, `useChatLane.ts:157`, `middleware.ts:30`), and the sign-in page draws its expiry message (S-05.06) only for `?timeout=true` (`Login.tsx:101-105`): have it read `sessionExpired` as well, or send `timeout=true`. The other paths keep the Phase 4 mapping.
- **Implementation plan (CSS capitals):** the lock's "no CSS capitals on labels or buttons" (`§ Never`) is still broken in live components. Remove `textTransform: "uppercase"` at `PrimaryButton.tsx:17` (every button it draws; `PrimaryButton.test.tsx:43` asserts `textTransform: 'uppercase'` for it, so that test changes in the same commit and asserts the new style instead), `ChatCore.tsx:1139` (the Experiment switch's label) and `ChatCore.tsx:1385` ("Current chat" and "New chat"), `ChatBrowser.tsx:95` ("Student chats"), `TopicsPanel.tsx:66` ("Topics"), `IntentsPanel.tsx:43` ("Student intent") and `ChatDetailDialog.tsx:331` ("AI answer · {model}"), and the chip's `textTransform: "lowercase"` at `ExperimentList.tsx:192`, which would turn ChatGPT into chatgpt. The strings are already in sentence case, so the type tokens (`§ Type`) draw them as written.
- **Implementation plan:** change copy and tests together, using the index's Tests column; add the date and number helpers and the `toLocale*String()` guard (a call with no locale, and one with any explicit non-English locale: `§ Dates, times and numbers`); map statuses to cause lines (`§ Errors`) and keep the generic cause line as the fallback only, shipping the sign-in mapping (S-05.04, S-05.05, S-05.16) in the same change as S-05.03; ship the index's New text in every native `alert()`, `confirm()` and `prompt()` call (the nine sites H3 lists; only the browser's own OK and Cancel buttons stay until a flow change, because C2 cannot reach them); implement the reserved no-permission strings with the B4 fix; ship the email copy to both files; delete or allowlist the orphaned files before the copy test runs; port `check-copy.mjs`'s lints (banned terms, exclamation marks, title case, `toLocale*`) as the copy sibling of the raw-colour test, so new strings cannot reintroduce what this phase removed.

## Chart tokens

**Phase 6 (2026-10-18), appended under the lock; the follow-up after review round 2 applied the same day** (the Opus review's 13 Minors and the second review's Major and Minors, recorded in the Phase 6 discovery note under `Follow-up after review round 2`). The data colours and the data rules: five categorical series and a neutral, registered under the reserved `chart.*` namespace; the encoding rules every chart follows; how the Phase 3 KPI tile and table component are applied to the analytics and admin data; and a text summary for every chart. It adds no global, changes no locked value and no Phase 2, 3, 4 or 5 wording. **It replaces the interim `palette.chart` mapping of `§ Design system › Generation spec` (the `accent-11`, `info-11`, `warning-11`, `neutral-11`, `accent-12` stand-ins, checker § 16):** that text stays as committed; the emitter now writes `palette.chart` from the tokens below, § 16 of `check-tokens.mjs` asserts them, and `Theme.test.ts`'s expectations move to these values (`§ Hand-offs`). Plan: `.design-foundations/plans/2026-10-16-kiwi-ui-stage-1.md`, Phase 6 (DW-6.1 to DW-6.7); brief OQ2; the tour's System Analytics and Class Analytics observations (TD-27, TD-28, TD-29, TD-36, TD-40, SF-28, SF-29, SF-44). Doctrine applied: data-viz (Tufte's data-ink; Knaflic's one preattentive attribute and colour for category, not quantity; Munzner's channels, hue for identity, position on a common scale first; Cleveland and McGill; Cairo's lies, the zero baseline, the stated range, a baseline or context for every KPI; Few's dashboard rules, one accent, status colours reserved; the colour-blind rule, redundant encoding, simulation), usability (Nielsen #1, #4 and #6; Gestalt figure and ground; WCAG 1.4.1, 1.4.11, 1.1.1) and the dataviz colour formula (four colour jobs, six checks, a runnable validator, a fixed slot order never cycled, text wears text tokens, status reserved, a legend for two or more series). Principle-derived choices are marked `[principle-derived]`.

### Registration

Registered in `.design-foundations/tokens/kiwi-ui.tokens.json` under the reserved `chart` group (never edited into the three tiers); every token names its CSS property, so the resolver's naming rule is untouched; the emitter writes them into `generated/kiwi-ui.tokens.css` (`:root`, and again in both colour blocks) and into `generated/mui-theme.json` as `palette.chart` (`series1`–`series5`, `other`, and the chrome roles `grid`, `baseline`, `tickText`, `plot`), no longer marked `_interim`. The series and the neutral are literals **derived, never typed**: each carries its OKLCH parameters (hue; L and C per mode) in `$extensions["org.kiwi"].derived`, `derive-chart.mjs` wrote them, and `check-tokens.mjs` § 16 re-renders the parameters and fails on any drift (§ 5 admits a `chart.*` literal only when it carries that derivation, as it admits a frozen `companion.*` literal). The chrome roles are references. No track is registered: the topic bars have none since the follow-up (`§ Encoding rules`), and the progress bar's track is Phase 3's `--in-progress-track`. § 16 fails on a `chart.*` role it does not know and asserts every series at 3:1 on a track as soon as one is registered; the retired `chart.track` (`--surface-active`) would have failed it for four of the five slots (series 2 and 5 at 2.80 and 2.87:1 in light, series 3 and 4 at 2.73 and 2.84:1 in dark).

| Token | CSS | Light | Dark | OKLCH (light / dark) | Use |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `chart.series.1` | `--chart-1` | `#2b774d` | `#3f895e` | 156° · L 0.511 C 0.101 / L 0.571 C 0.100 | **The accent hue, the one accent of every chart.** Every single-series chart draws in it: the two time series and Top actions on A-03, the intent bars and the topic bars on A-18, and slot 1 of every categorical view |
| `chart.series.2` | `--chart-2` | `#0098af` | `#00b2cc` | 213° · L 0.625 C 0.109 / L 0.701 C 0.123 | Categorical slot 2 (teal) |
| `chart.series.3` | `--chart-3` | `#a64e00` | `#b65a15` | 51° · L 0.526 C 0.137 / L 0.570 C 0.140 | Categorical slot 3 (amber) |
| `chart.series.4` | `--chart-4` | `#0064ad` | `#3e7ab4` | 250° · L 0.495 C 0.140 / L 0.566 C 0.110 | Categorical slot 4 (blue) |
| `chart.series.5` | `--chart-5` | `#958b00` | `#a99d15` | 104° · L 0.626 C 0.132 / L 0.685 C 0.140 | Categorical slot 5 (olive) |
| `chart.other` | `--chart-other` | `#77746d` | `#8d8b83` | 94° · L 0.560 C 0.012 / L 0.635 C 0.012 | The neutral for "Other" and "Unknown": the neutral ramp's hue and chroma at the mode's target lightness, so it carries the same weight as the five and reads as de-emphasis; always with its label, never the only category |
| `chart.grid` | `--chart-grid` | `{semantic.color.border-subtle}` | same | gridlines: solid hairlines one step off the surface, never dashed |
| `chart.baseline` | `--chart-baseline` | `{semantic.color.border-strong}` | same | the zero line and the axis rule; bars grow from it |
| `chart.tick-text` | `--chart-tick-text` | `{semantic.color.text-secondary}` | same | axis ticks, legend text, direct labels: text wears text tokens, never a series colour |
| `chart.plot` | `--chart-plot` | `{semantic.color.surface}` | same | the plot background, the sheet a chart sits on |

**Contrast (DW-6.1).** Every series clears 3:1 on the plot background, the canvas and the hover row in both modes, by measurement (`check-tokens.mjs` § 16; the full table is in `generated/contrast-report.md`): light, on `--surface` `#faf9f3` / `--background` `#f4f1e4` / `--surface-hover` `#f1f0ed`: series 1 5.17 / 4.82 / 4.79, series 2 3.26 / 3.04 / 3.02, series 3 5.36 / 5.00 / 4.97, series 4 5.81 / 5.41 / 5.38, series 5 3.33 / 3.11 / 3.09, the neutral 4.09 at least; dark, on `--surface` `#252218` / `--background` `#1b1915` / `--surface-hover` `#2b2a27`: 3.75 / 4.14 / 3.38, 6.24 / 6.89 / 5.63, 3.38 / 3.73 / 3.05, 3.52 / 3.88 / 3.18, 5.69 / 6.29 / 5.14, the neutral 4.21 at least. The lowest is 3.02:1 (series 2, light, on the hover row).

**Matched lightness, as measured (DW-6.1).** The five sit within **± 0.07 of one target lightness per mode** (light 0.560, spread 0.130; dark 0.634, spread 0.135), and the checker asserts the band. The plan's "matched OKLCH lightness" is given this tolerance for a measured reason, recorded here so a review can weigh it: at one exact lightness per mode, five hue-separated series cannot be both colour-blind-safe and quiet. Searched exhaustively over the admissible hues (a grid search of 66,045 hue sets at matched lightness, recorded in the Phase 6 discovery note, then the seeded derivation, which reproduces the failing figures when its band or chroma constants are tightened): at one lightness the best colour-blind separation at chroma ≤ 0.14 is 6.3 (the floor band, legal only with secondary encoding), 8.2 is reached only at chroma 0.22 (vivid, outside the muted pin) with a full-colour separation of 8.7, and the full-colour floor of 15 is unreachable at any chroma, because lightness is the channel dichromats keep; at ± 0.06 the colour-blind gate still fails at chroma ≤ 0.14 (7.9) and passes at chroma 0.15 by 0.09; **± 0.07 at chroma ≤ 0.14 passes both gates with a margin** (8.14 / 15.26 light, 8.16 / 15.37 dark). The five interim stand-ins measured 0.8 under deuteranopia. A tighter band fails the colour-blind clause of the same requirement, a vivid set breaks the pinned chroma, and fewer series changes the plan's scope, so the band is the one admissible reading, not a taste direction; the swatch sheet and the colour-vision strips below are the rendered evidence for anyone who wants to weigh it by eye. `[principle-derived: the dataviz formula's lightness band (0.43–0.77) applied as narrowly as the gates allow]`

### Derivation

`node .design-foundations/tokens/derive-chart.mjs` (deterministic; seed 20261018; `--dry` prints without writing) is the regeneration procedure for `chart.*`, as the three `palette.mjs` runs are for the locked block. Fixed by the lock: series 1 is the pinned accent hue (156°); the plot surfaces are the locked `--surface`, `--background` and `--surface-hover` of each mode; the admissible hue arc is 45°–255°, outside purple and violet (265°–345°, `§ Never`), pink and magenta (345°–15°, the brief) and red (15°–45°, `§ Functional colour usage`: never in analytical views); chroma stays at or below 0.14 (muted: between the accent's 0.095 and the balanced functional colours' 0.11–0.19) and at or above 0.10 (below it a hue reads as grey). Searched: the four other hues on the arc, one lightness and one chroma per series per mode, the lightnesses within ± 0.07 of one target per mode. Objective: the minimum pairwise OKLab distance (× 100) under protanopia and deuteranopia (the Machado, Oliveira and Fernandes 2009 transforms at severity 1.0, all ten pairs, both modes), then the minimum under normal vision; the gates are 8 and 15 (the dataviz colour formula's target and full-colour floor). Order: slots 2–5 take the permutation that maximises the minimum **adjacent** distance (stacks, bars and lines meet only their neighbours): 14.78 light, 16.45 dark. Every parameter is recorded in the group's `$extensions["org.kiwi"].derivation` (gates, surfaces, model, the measured minima) and on each token (`derived`); the checker re-renders and re-asserts all of it, so the palette is reproducible from its record.

**Colour-vision evidence (DW-6.1).** Minimum pairwise distance after simulation, all ten pairs: **light 8.14** (protanopia, series 1 vs 3), **dark 8.16** (deuteranopia, series 1 vs 3); under normal vision 15.26 (light, 1 vs 2) and 15.37 (dark, 1 vs 4); the neutral is 4.66 (light) and 5.57 (dark) from its nearest series under simulation, informational: Other is de-emphasis and always carries its label. On the Model choice donut that nearest pair is ChatGPT (`--chart-1`) and Unknown (`--chart-other`): 4.66 under protanopia and 5.48 under deuteranopia in light, 5.57 and 6.98 in dark, so the hue alone does not tell them apart, and every slice carries its name as a direct label (`§ Encoding rules › Donuts`). The independent check, the dataviz validator (`scripts/validate_palette.js … --pairs all`, the same model), passes every check in light and every distance, chroma and contrast check in dark; its one dark FAIL is its generic lightness band (0.48–0.67), calibrated to a darker surface than the lock's paper-3, which needs L ≥ 0.53 for 3:1 and so puts the band's centre higher. **The strip:** `.design-foundations/mocks/screenshots/chart-sheet.cvd-none.png`, `chart-sheet.cvd-deuteranopia.png` and `chart-sheet.cvd-protanopia.png` are the sheet's five-series strip rendered by headless Chrome 155 with `Emulation.setEmulatedVisionDeficiency` (the DevTools "Emulate vision deficiencies" rendering, an acceptable equivalent of Coblis), the pairwise minima printed on the strip from the rendered tokens; they go into the Phase 7 sign-off materials with the swatch sheet.

### Colour rules

- **Categorical colour appears only where the marks are categories (DW-6.4):** the Model choice donut (ChatGPT `--chart-1`, Qwen `--chart-2`, Unknown `--chart-other`) and the User duration donut (Chat `--chart-1`, Scratchpad `--chart-2`), where every slice also carries its name as a direct label, so no category is told by its colour alone (WCAG 1.4.1; `§ Encoding rules › Donuts`). Every other chart in Stage 1 is one series, so it draws in `--chart-1` alone: both time series and Top actions on System analytics, the intent bars and the topic bars on Class analytics. A single-series bar chart never colours its bars by category or by value: the category is on the axis, and a value ramp on nominal categories spends the identity channel on what length already shows. `[principle-derived: Munzner's expressiveness; Knaflic's one attribute]`
- **One accent per view:** `--chart-1` is the accent hue at the chart lightness; `--accent-solid` stays the control colour (forest signifies interactive, Norman), `--accent-text` the link colour, and neither is a data mark.
- **The slot order is fixed and never cycled:** a category keeps its slot across filters and ranges (colour follows the entity, never its rank); a sixth category never gets a sixth hue: it folds into Other (`--chart-other`) or the view re-encodes (a sorted bar chart, a table). "Other" and "Unknown" are always the neutral.
- **Functional hues as categories, kept apart by step and by place:** a series may share a family with a functional colour (green, blue, yellow), and the two never meet: chart colours are the chart steps only (neither `-9` nor `-11` of any ramp), appear only as marks inside a plot that carries a legend or a direct label, and never carry a status meaning; functional colours appear only on status surfaces with an icon and a label (`§ Functional colour usage`) and never as a data mark; red is excluded from charts outright. A series that would *mean* good or bad wears status tokens with an icon and a label; none exists in Stage 1.
- **Text wears text tokens:** ticks, legends, direct labels and summaries are `--chart-tick-text`, `--text-secondary` or `--text`, never a series colour (the `tokens.ts` monospace counts in violet go).
- **No sequential or diverging ramp is registered:** no Stage 1 view encodes magnitude in colour. The rule for a future one, so it is not invented ad hoc: one hue, the accent (156°), light to dark, the lightest step at least 2:1 on the plot; a diverging scale would pair the blue slot and the amber slot around the neutral, never two cool hues, never a hue at the midpoint.
- **No track is registered:** a bar that ranks (the topic, intent and action bars) has no track behind it (`§ Encoding rules › Inline bars in a ranked list`), and the progress bar's track is Phase 3's `--in-progress-track`. A future meter, a value against a real maximum such as a quota, registers `chart.track`, and `check-tokens.mjs` § 16 then asserts every series at 3:1 on it.

### Encoding rules (DW-6.2)

Applied in every chart spec of `kiwi-ui/JOURNEY.md` (the `Data surfaces (Phase 6)` bullets of A-03 and A-18) and visible on the chart sheet.

- **Counts take integer ticks:** a Y axis that counts people, sessions, questions or interactions never shows 0.25 or 0.75 (TD-36): `allowDecimals={false}`, ticks at steps of 1, 2, 5 or 10, thousands with a comma.
- **No smoothing, no gradient fill:** a daily series is bars; an hourly series is a 2 px straight-segment line with no area fill; a daily series whose buckets would fall below 4 px each (a range longer than about 150 days at 1280 px) also draws as a line; never `type="monotone"`, never a `linearGradient`, never a `fillOpacity` wash (a smoothed curve invents values between counts; a gradient is chartjunk).
- **Bars grow from zero:** `domain={[0, 'auto']}`; the zero line is a 1 px `--chart-baseline` rule; a bar is at most 24 px thick, with a 4 px radius at its data end only and square at the baseline, and a 2 px `--chart-plot` gap separates touching bars and donut slices (the gap does the separating, never a stroke around a mark).
- **Units on axes, the range stated:** the chart's title names the measure (Phase 5's names); the line under it, the range line, states the unit, the bucket and the period, and the period always carries its dates: a custom range is its dates ("Users per day, 1 Oct – 7 Oct 2026"; a range inside one day is the date, "Sessions per hour, 17 Oct 2026"), and a preset is its sentence form with its dates in brackets after it ("Users per day, the last 7 days (1 Oct – 7 Oct 2026)"; rows P6-A-03: the sentence forms of A-03.33 to A-03.40 and the bracketed dates), because the X ticks leave the year out and a relative period alone dates nothing once the page is shared or printed; the period button keeps the preset's own label. In a sentence the period follows "for", which reads with every form ("for the last 7 days", "for 1 Oct – 7 Oct 2026", "for 17 Oct 2026", where "in 17 Oct 2026" would not), so one summary template serves a preset, a custom range and a single day (rows P6-A-03); a summary does not repeat a preset's dates, which the range line above the plot states. Class analytics has no date range, so its charts' second line states the scope instead ("Questions by intent, all time"; "Questions by intent in this lecture, all time"; rows P6-A-18). The value axis names its unit where the title does not, and the X axis reads the C3 forms: `1 Oct` for a day, `14:00` for an hour (the year is left out because the range line states it, which `§ Dates, times and numbers` allows), `1 Oct 2026, 14:05` in the tooltip (`chartXAxis.ts:50`, `:80`, `:85`, `:91`, each a Phase 6 row; the date guard has no `en-US` display call left to allow). An hourly axis keeps one rhythm from midnight (every 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 or 12 hours, the axis helper's intervals), and the last bucket gets no tick of its own: a day's 24 buckets are ticked at 00:00 (the date, `17 Oct`), 06:00, 12:00 and 18:00, never at 23:00.
- **Grid and chrome recede:** horizontal gridlines only on a column or line chart, solid 1 px `--chart-grid` (never dashed); a horizontal bar chart draws the zero baseline and no vertical gridlines, so nothing runs through a tip label, and no value axis where its bars carry their values (`Legends and labels`); a direct label that can cross a gridline (a peak label) is haloed in `--chart-plot`; no axis line besides the baseline; ticks and labels in `--chart-tick-text` at `--type-caption`; the plot sits on `--chart-plot` with no border and no shadow (figure and ground: data is the figure).
- **Legends and labels:** a legend whenever two or more series are drawn, with each entry's value and unit; a single series has no legend box, the title names it. Direct labels go where the chart is read, and only there: the extreme of a time series (its highest bucket, daily and hourly alike, with its value), the tip of every bar of a sorted bar chart, every slice of a donut; never a number on every point of a series (a near-empty series, below, labels each of its one or two bars). Where every bar carries its value the value axis is not drawn, because its ticks would only repeat the labels: Top actions and the intents are their labels and the zero baseline. The category gutter of a horizontal bar chart is as wide as its longest label, measured from the rendered text, so no label spills or clips ("Code and output sent to chat" on Top actions). A tooltip supplements and never gates the reading: the figures a chart is read for (a time series' total and its extreme, every bar of a sorted bar chart, every share of a part-to-whole) are on the chart or in its summary, and any other bucket reads against the integer value axis; the tooltip gives that bucket's exact value, and a table-view twin that lists every value is Stage 2 (`§ Hand-offs`).
- **Donuts:** only for part-to-whole with two to four parts of very different size (both A-03 donuts qualify); five or more parts or close values take a sorted bar chart instead. Every slice carries a direct label, its name over its share (`ChatGPT` over `66%`, row P6-A-03), outside the ring beside the slice's middle and never inside a thin slice; a slice under 10% is joined to its label by a 1 px leader in `--chart-tick-text`, the label's own colour, which clears 3:1 where the `--chart-baseline` hairline (2.18:1 light, 2.53:1 dark on the plot) would not. So no slice is told by its colour alone (WCAG 1.4.1): under colour-vision simulation `--chart-1` and `--chart-other` sit 4.66 apart in light and 5.57 in dark (protanopia), below the 8 the series keep between them, and the label, not the hue, names Unknown. The legend lists each part's value with its unit (`{n: count request}`; `about {hours: count hour}`), the centre the total with its unit, the labels the shares: each figure has one place. **Shares drawn together sum to 100%:** a donut's labels, and the intent bars' labels, are whole percents by largest-remainder rounding (each share floored, the points left over going one each to the largest remainders, a tie to the earlier slot), and the summary quotes the same figures, so Model choice reads 66%, 31% and 3%, never 66%, 31% and 4% (101%). The legend's hours say "about", as the summary does, because they are rounded from an estimate whose minutes also give the shares: "about 20 hours" (62%) and "about 13 hours" (38%) agree with "33 hours" within the rounding, where bare figures would not (20 of 33 is 61%). **One unit per chart:** User duration is in hours, to two significant figures (`33 hours`, `4.5 hours`, `0.33 hours`; never more digits, and never 0 for a part that is not 0), in the centre, the legend, the tooltip and the summary, because its minutes are an estimate from action counts and a decimal on 32.9 hours claims a precision they do not have; the legend's minutes (A-03.22) go (rows P6-A-03).
- **Sorted bars for ranking:** a nominal-category comparison is a horizontal bar chart sorted by count, "Other" last, the category name as the row label, in one colour, from the zero baseline with no track (Top actions; the intents). Each bar's label is the figure its panel is read for: Top actions gives its counts (`1,234`), the intents their shares (`{share}%`, row P6-A-18), the bar's length then being the share, with the intents' counts in the cards below, in the summary and in each bar's tooltip (P6-A-18.07); neither chart draws a value axis, so a label never disagrees with an axis.
- **Inline bars in a ranked list (the topics):** each bar grows from a common zero at the row's start, on one scale set by the largest count, with no track behind it. A track scaled to the largest topic reads as a whole that each bar fills a share of, and the top topic as a full one; a track scaled to the total would turn the bars into shares of every question, which is not what the list ranks. The count beside each bar is its value (`{n: count question}`), in `--text`, tabular.
- **Empty and near-empty data:** a chart whose series is empty shows the empty-state line in the plot's place (`No activity in this date range`, `No model use in this date range`, `No analyzed questions yet`), never a zero-filled curve, six zero bars or an empty ring; a series with one or two non-zero buckets draws them as bars, each labelled with its value, with no line and no value axis, since its bars carry their values (`Legends and labels`; the zero baseline and the X ticks stay) (the user's decision of 2026-10-18): the days of a daily series, and the hours of a 24-bucket hourly series too, whose one or two non-zero hours draw as bars in their slots, never as a line along zero.
- **Motion:** a chart appears with the page; there is no entrance animation (`chartAnimation.ts`'s 1,000 ms growth of bars and areas goes: the locked budget allows opacity and transform only, 150–220 ms, never an animated width or height, and one mover per view). On a data refresh the previous render holds at reduced opacity and the new one replaces it with an opacity transition inside the budget (`--motion-standard`), the chart as a whole being the one mover; no skeleton flash. Under reduced motion the swap is immediate.
- **In progress:** while topics are being analyzed the panel shows the Phase 3 in-progress alert (the ring icon, "Analyzing student questions…", A-18.06, and the text dots) and no progress bar. The analysis reports no fraction (`topics.generating` is a flag, `TopicsPanel.tsx:44`), so a filled bar would be a made-up figure (C13), and an indeterminate sweep or pulse is outside the locked motion budget (`§ Motion`; `§ Design system › Generation spec` retires `.kiwi-progress-bar-indeterminate` for that reason); the text dots are the view's one mover and hold still under reduced motion. The alert's LinearProgress (Phase 3) is drawn only where the work reports a real fraction.

### KPI tiles (DW-6.3)

The Phase 3 tile applied: label in `--type-label`, value in `--type-kpi-value` (`1,234`; `{n} min`), the icon always `--icon-default` (TD-27: one colour rule on both pages), and a third line, in `--type-caption` `--text-secondary`, that is a baseline, a trend or the statement that there is none. A trend carries the Phase 3 tile's direction glyph, in the line's own colour (`--kpi-tile-trend`) and hidden from assistive technology, and states its direction in words, so neither the glyph nor a colour carries it; it is never a status colour, because more questions is neither good nor bad. Every third line is data the page already fetches. On System analytics a prior-period comparison would be a second request (the analytics API returns the chosen range only) and is a Stage 2 item; on Class analytics the weekly series is already fetched (`weeklyActivity`, grouped by week at `class-analytics.service.ts:177-183`), so the Questions tile carries a real week-on-week trend.

| Page | Tile | Value | Third line |
|---|---|---|---|
| A-03 | Active users | the range's unique users | "No earlier period to compare" (P6-A-03): the API returns the chosen range only |
| A-03 | Sessions | the sessions started in the range | the same |
| A-03 | Interactions | the recorded actions in the range | the same |
| A-03 | Average session | `{n} min` | the same; the label already says average |
| A-18 | Questions | the analyzed questions (scoped to the lecture when one is chosen) | a week-on-week trend from `weeklyActivity` (fetched today, never drawn), the direction glyph before it: "Last full week: 12 questions, up from 9 the week before" (P6-A-18.01), "…, down from 9 the week before" (P6-A-18.17) or "…, the same as the week before" (P6-A-18.18). The weeks are the backend's UTC Mondays (`date_trunc('week')`, sent as the UTC instant of Monday 00:00): the last full week is the latest Monday-to-Sunday week that has ended in UTC, the week before is seven days earlier, and a week missing from the series counts as 0 (the GROUP BY returns no row for a week without questions). No percentage: a change between small counts reads larger than it is, and a week from 0 has none. "No earlier period to compare" when the week before falls before the series' first week, when the series is empty, and while a lecture is chosen (the value is then the lecture's count, the weekly series the class's) |
| A-18 | Active students | the count | "No earlier period to compare" |
| A-18 | Topics | the count | the same |
| A-18 | Active lectures / Last activity | `{active}` (the ratio's numerator; today's `3/12` goes) / the date (A-18.04) | "Of 12 lectures in the class" (the ratio's denominator is its baseline) / "No earlier period to compare" |

The four A-03 tiles repeat one line ("No earlier period to compare"). A single line for the row would say it once (Nielsen #8), but the Phase 3 tile carries its own third line (`§ Design system › KPI tiles`: a trend or baseline line, or "no baseline"), so moving the statement to the row would change the Phase 3 tile. The repetition stays in Stage 1; the row-level line is a Stage 2 change to the tile (`§ Hand-offs`).

### Tables (DW-6.5)

The Phase 3 table component (`§ Tables`, `§ DataGrid (MUI X) mapping`) applied to every admin table; each page's `Data surfaces (Phase 6)` bullet in `kiwi-ui/JOURNEY.md` names its columns (A-03 ×2, A-05, A-06, A-07, A-08 ×4, A-09 ×2, A-10 ×2, A-11 ×2, A-14, A-15, A-16, A-22 ×3, A-23, A-24), the tables inside overlays included (A-03's token usage detail, A-09's delivery history, A-11's registration results). The rules read against the data:

- **Numbers:** a quantity (tokens, sessions, interactions, order, priority, a count) is a right-aligned tabular numeral in `--text`, `1,234`, at `--size-col-min-num`; the unit has one place per column: in the header ("Tokens", "Interactions", "Priority") with the cells bare numerals, or in the cell by the count helper (`{n: count class}`, `{n} min`) under a header that names the column, never both (the Token usage detail's Interactions column keeps its unit in the header, so its cells become numerals: row P6-A-03 supersedes A-03.52's New); a version string or an id is text, left-aligned, monospace where it is an identifier, at the scale's code size (`--type-code-size`, 14 px).
- **Dates:** `17 Oct 2026, 14:05` at `--size-col-min-date`; an audit log keeps the seconds and its header says "(local time)"; no value is the em dash named "No value" (DS.53, DS.54).
- **Column widths:** a column takes its content's width where the container allows: a date column `--size-col-min-date`, a number column the wider of its header and `--size-col-min-num`, a status column `--size-col-min-status`, and the text column the rest, so the text column gives way first, down to `--size-col-min-text`, and only then truncates; below the sum of the minimums the table scrolls in its own container (Phase 3). The Token usage detail shows a whole session id in its dialog at full width (900 px) and cuts it at an 800 px viewport (736 px), where the dates and numbers keep their widths.
- **Truncation:** an email, a name, an identifier, a URL or a subject is one line with an ellipsis and the full text on hover and focus (the truncated span is focusable and carries the text as its accessible description), at `--size-col-min-text` or more; a cell whose text fits shows it whole, with no tab stop and no tooltip, and its span becomes focusable and gains the tooltip only while its text is cut (measured, `scrollWidth > clientWidth`, again on resize); the eight-character ids with "…" and the 300 px cut go, and so does the three-line slug. The full text is Phase 3's tooltip (`MuiTooltip`): `--type-caption` on `--surface` with the toggletip geometry (at most `--toggletip-max-width` wide and wrapping inside it, an identifier breaking anywhere; `--toggletip-padding-y` and `-x`, `--toggletip-radius`, `--toggletip-shadow`), set `--toggletip-offset` below its span so that it never covers the span's focus ring, and in the cell's face (monospace for an identifier); it opens on hover and on focus, the pointer can move onto it, and Escape dismisses it without moving focus (WCAG 1.4.13). The pointer reaches it across the offset because the offset belongs to the tooltip's own box: MUI draws it as the tooltip's margin inside its popper (its default for a bottom placement), never as a Popper `offset` modifier, which leaves a gap that belongs to neither the span nor the tooltip, so the tooltip closes on the way; failing that, a `leaveDelay` long enough to cross the gap. The chart sheet draws the same bridge (`.tip::before` over the offset, and the hovered span's `::after` under it), and a pointer moved from the span to the tip in 1 px steps never sees it close. Escape dismisses only a tooltip whose span is hovered or focused, and after it the next hover or focus opens it again. The span's ring is the table's focus state: 2 px `--focus-ring`, inset −2 px.
- **Wrap columns:** one designated column may clamp at two lines (a description, a data preview, a reason, a chunk's text, a prompt's instructions), with the full text on hover and focus, except the JSON of a webhook event: A-10's Data preview defers its full text to the details overlay ("Event data", A-10.15), opened from the row, and has no tooltip, because a JSON document is a reading surface (A-10's density override) and not a tooltip's text. This is the Phase 3 wrap-column exception (`§ Design system › Tables`: designated wrap columns clamp at two lines instead of truncating to one).
- **Status:** a status cell is the status chip with its icon at `--size-col-min-status`; a role cell is the neutral role chip; a boolean is a switch with an accessible name or a chip, never a bare dot.
- **Tables on the canvas:** rules, not cards; no literal header fill (`#f5f5f5` goes); the footer and pagination follow the DataGrid footer rules; loading is skeleton rows, empty is the empty-state frame, errors are the alert with Retry in the body slot.

### Text summaries (DW-6.6)

Every chart has a text summary: a sentence in the Exact and Steady voice, computed from the data the chart draws, that is the figure's visible caption (`--type-caption`, under the plot) and its accessible description. The figure is the one element named and described: `aria-labelledby` its title, `aria-describedby` its caption, and the chart's SVG is `aria-hidden="true"` with no name of its own, so the name and the summary are announced once (Recharts 3 gives every chart SVG a tab stop and the `application` role through `accessibilityLayer`, which is on by default, so each chart sets `accessibilityLayer={false}`; `§ Hand-offs`). A chart whose marks are controls keeps them reachable: the intent bars are buttons (SF-29), drawn in HTML, so nothing focusable sits inside a hidden SVG. Pattern: the measure with its period ("for the last 7 days") or its scope ("in this lecture"), a colon, the key figure first (a count by the helper, with its unit), then the second figure; a fact the counts give (a total, a peak, the largest share), never a trend claim ("improving", "healthy") the data cannot back (C16). A figure says what it counts: the active-users summary counts "different users" (a user active on several days counts once, so the figure is not the sum of the bars), the topics summary leads with the number of topics and gives the top topic its unit ("with 48 questions"). There are 11 summaries for the seven charts (the two time series and the two Class-analytics charts have two readings each), rows P6-A-03 and P6-A-18 under `## Revision index › Rows appended by later phases`; `check-copy.mjs` Q5 asserts the pattern. When a series is empty, the empty-state line is the figure's whole text and no summary is drawn.

### Evidence

- **Tokens and contrast:** `node .design-foundations/tokens/check-tokens.mjs` exits 0 (2026-10-18): § 16 asserts the five series and the neutral in both modes at ≥ 3:1 on the three surfaces, the ± 0.07 band, the hue arc, the chroma bounds, the colour-blind and full-colour minima, every hex equal to its parameters re-rendered, the chrome references and the MUI preview, and, since the follow-up, that every `chart.*` role is a known one and that a track, if one is registered, holds every series at 3:1; the report is `generated/contrast-report.md § 16`, the counts `generated/check-summary.json` (`chart`). § 1 still proves the lock (114 of 114 aliases equal).
- **Independent colour check:** the dataviz validator, both modes, all pairs (`§ Derivation`).
- **Render:** `.design-foundations/mocks/chart-sheet.html`, self-contained and offline (the emitter's CSS pasted verbatim as its first style block; the self-hosted Source Sans 3 at `assets/fonts/source-sans-3.css`; hand-drawn SVG, no library): the five series and the neutral with their measures, one example per analytics view with the encoding rules applied (daily bars, an hourly line, Top actions, both donuts, the intent bars, the topic bars with the in-progress alert), the KPI tile in its three forms, an admin table sample with numerals, units and a truncated cell, and the empty state; the sheet audits itself (contrast and pairwise distances from the rendered tokens, written onto the strip). Screenshots: `screenshots/chart-sheet.1440x900.png`, `chart-sheet.1440x900.dark.png`, `chart-sheet.1440.full.png`, `chart-sheet.1440.full.dark.png`; the colour-vision strips `chart-sheet.cvd-none.png`, `chart-sheet.cvd-deuteranopia.png`, `chart-sheet.cvd-protanopia.png` and the table captures `chart-sheet.table-focus.png` / `.dark.png` (the first identifier focused by a Tab key, its tooltip open, the focus ring showing); all by `node .design-foundations/tokens/shoot-chart-sheet.mjs`, which also measures the sheet 1:1 and writes `generated/chart-sheet-audit.json`: the SVG scale (1.0), the bar thickness (at most 24 px), the line (2 px), the hairlines (1 px), the chart text (12 px), the tile icon (`--kpi-tile-icon-size`), and the tooltip hit-test (the element under the tip's centre is the tip, drawn open, on hover and on keyboard focus). Since the follow-up after review round 2 it also records, in both modes: every donut slice named by a direct label inside its figure, no two labels overlapping and none over the ring (the Model choice pair ChatGPT and Unknown, 4.66 apart in light and 5.57 in dark under simulation, is told apart by its labels), with the two donuts captured under the same emulation (`chart-sheet.donuts.cvd-none.png`, `chart-sheet.donuts.cvd-deuteranopia.png`, `chart-sheet.donuts.cvd-protanopia.png`, each with a `.dark` twin); the bar charts' gutters (154 px for Top actions' longest label of 145.5 px, 112 px for the intents' 103.5 px; no label left of the plot) and no value tick on either; the hourly ticks (`17 Oct`, `06:00`, `12:00`, `18:00`: one rhythm); the extremes labelled (21 on the daily bars, 9 on the hourly line); 9 of 9 figures named and described and 7 of 7 chart SVGs hidden and unnamed; the topic bars (no track, each width the count over the largest); no progress bar; the swatch captions at 5.70:1 (light) and 7.92:1 (dark) at the least, on the sheet and never on a series colour; and the tooltip (12 px in the cell's monospace, the cell at 14 px; at most 320 px wide, a 388-character text wrapping onto 10 lines; 8 px below its span, whose ring is inset −2 px; Escape hides it with the focus kept, and the next focus brings it back). Since the re-review it also walks a real pointer from a truncated span to its tooltip and along it in 1, 4, 8 and 12 px steps (the tooltip never closes on the way, rows 1 and 2, both modes; the reviewer's own pointer scripts agree), presses Escape at load and then Tab (the tooltip shows on the focused span), and records the Token usage detail at both dialog widths (at 900 px nothing is cut and no cell takes a tab stop, the id column 393 px; at 736 px all three ids are cut, each a tab stop, the id column 229 px, the dates 136 px and the numbers 97 and 88 px, the compact density's minimums and the Interactions header), the Questions tile's trend ("Last full week: 12 questions, up from 9 the week before", its glyph hidden and in the line's colour), the near-empty chart with no value tick and no gridline, and the Model choice legend's 4 px row gap. After the second re-review the sheet's demo-open tooltip (row 1, its static figure of the open state) closes on the first focus or pointer in the table, so a Tab never lands on a span it covers (Tab twice from the top: row 2's span focused, its ring showing and nothing over it); an Escape at load closes that figure and dismisses no span, so the first hover opens the tooltip; and the sheet redraws and re-audits itself when `data-theme` changes, so its numbers always match the chips (the shoot script switches modes that way).

### Hand-offs

- **Phase 7 (Mocks):** the six screens contain no analytics view, so the sign-off materials include the chart sheet's screenshots, the three colour-vision strips and the donuts' colour-vision captures beside the Phase 2 swatch sheet; a mock that draws a chart pastes the same CSS and uses `--chart-*` only. **A Phase 7 design call (review round 1, F12; the user decides):** on System analytics one green carries three meanings, the single-series accent (`--chart-1`), slot 1 of the donuts (Chat; ChatGPT) and, as `--accent-*`, the controls. Phase 7 decides whether slot 1 of a categorical view should skip to slot 2 so that the accent hue stays single-series only; the tokens do not change either way. The rest of F12 is settled by the follow-up after review round 2: every donut slice carries its name and share as a direct label, so ChatGPT and Unknown, 4.66 apart under simulation, are told apart in words, and User duration has one unit.
- **Implementation plan:**
  - `Theme.ts`: `palette.chart` from the generated theme (`series1`–`series5`, `other`, `grid`, `baseline`, `tickText`, `plot`; the `ChartPalette` type gains the new keys); `Theme.test.ts` expects these values, replacing the interim stand-ins; `ClassAnalytics/tokens.ts` is retired (its violet, intent hues, amber, red and the monospace counts).
  - The two area charts (`StandaloneUserActivity.tsx`, `StandaloneDailySessions.tsx`): `BarChart` for daily buckets, a `Line` with `type="linear"` and no `Area` for hourly; `allowDecimals={false}` and `domain={[0, 'auto']}` on the Y axis; `CartesianGrid` solid in `--chart-grid`, horizontal only; the range line (a preset in its sentence form with its dates in brackets), the summary after "for" as the `<figcaption>`; the `<figure>` carries `aria-labelledby` (the title) and `aria-describedby` (the caption), and the chart sits in an `aria-hidden="true"` wrapper with `accessibilityLayer={false}` (Recharts 3 turns it on: a tab stop and the `application` role on the SVG); the highest bucket's value as a direct label (a `LabelList` on that bar, a labelled `ReferenceDot` on the hourly line); the hourly ticks on one rhythm from midnight, with no tick of their own for the last bucket; the stand-in subtitle strings go (rows P6-A-03). `chartAnimation.ts` and every `{...chartAnimation}` spread go with them (`isAnimationActive={false}`): the locked motion budget has no entrance animation; a refresh cross-fades the chart as one mover within `--motion-standard`.
  - `StandaloneTopActions.tsx`: one fill (`--chart-1`), sorted data with the two Other buckets last, the count label at the tip (`LabelList`); no value axis (`XAxis hide`), the zero baseline; the label gutter (`YAxis width`, a fixed 72 today, `StandaloneTopActions.tsx:216`) set from the longest label as rendered; the per-action colour map goes.
  - The donuts (`StandaloneUserDuration.tsx`, `StandaloneUserEngine.tsx`): slots 1 and 2 and the neutral, the stroke in `--chart-plot`; a direct label on every slice, its name over its share (`Pie` `label`, with `labelLine` in `--chart-tick-text` for a slice under 10%); the legend with each value and its unit and without the shares; "Scratchpad" for the second activity; User duration in hours to two significant figures in the centre, the legend and the tooltip (`StandaloneUserDuration.tsx:130`, `:117`, `:304`; rows P6-A-03).
  - `chartXAxis.ts`: the four formats from the fixed month table and the 24-hour clock (`formatDate`, `formatDateTime` helpers of `§ Dates, times and numbers`); the tests in `chartXAxis.test.ts` move with them; the date guard then has no `en-US` display call to allow.
  - `IntentsPanel.tsx`: the stacked bar becomes the sorted bar chart, drawn in HTML: a real button per bar holding the intent's name, the bar and its share label, its tooltip the count (`{n: count question}`, P6-A-18.07, in place of the name, count and share at `IntentsPanel.tsx:80`), and no value axis; the cards lose their colour squares, `QuestionListDialog`'s `accentColor` becomes `--chart-1`.
  - `TopicsPanel.tsx`: the inline bar in `--chart-1` from a common zero, with no track; while topics are analyzed, the in-progress alert with its text dots and no `LinearProgress` (the indeterminate bar at `TopicsPanel.tsx:96-103` goes).
  - `Analytics.tsx` and `ClassAnalytics.tsx`: the tile's third line (the rows; the Questions tile's week-on-week trend from `weeklyActivity`: the weeks keyed by their UTC Monday (`weekStart`, read with `getUTC*` or `timeZone: "UTC"`, never local time), the last full week and the one before it, a missing week counted as 0, the direction glyph from the icon set in the line's colour with `aria-hidden`, and "No earlier period to compare" when the week before precedes the series, when it is empty and while a lecture is chosen; `{active}` and its denominator line).
  - `StandaloneTopTokenUsers.tsx`: the list becomes the table; the dialog's "Usage" header reads "Interactions" and its cells become bare numerals (row P6-A-03 supersedes A-03.52); truncation with the focusable span whose clipped text and tooltip are siblings, under `table-layout: fixed` so the column widths hold; the tooltip is `MuiTooltip` with the toggletip tokens (its maximum width, wrapping, the offset below the span drawn as the tooltip's margin inside the popper, so the pointer can cross it; or a `leaveDelay`; verified with a pointer moved in 1 px steps), in the cell's monospace for the session id, hoverable and dismissed by Escape without moving focus, and the span's focus ring is inset −2 px; the columns take their content's widths, so the id is cut, and gains its tab stop and tooltip, only when the dialog is narrower than it needs (`§ Tables`).
  - Every admin table per its `Data surfaces (Phase 6)` bullet (right-aligned tabular numerals, units, the focusable truncated spans with the tooltip of `§ Tables`, the wrap columns, the status chips with icons), the tables inside overlays included (A-03's token usage detail, A-09's delivery history, A-11's registration results). A-09's delivery history logs a failed load to the console only and then reads as empty (`WebhookManagement.tsx:490-493`): its error state needs an error line, which no row writes yet (Stage 2 copy).
  - Stage 2: a table-view twin for each chart (the dataviz method's accessibility pass asks for one; the text summary and the legend satisfy the plugin doctrine's text alternative today); a prior-period request for the KPI baselines; one row-level "No earlier period to compare" line in place of the four on A-03 (a change to the Phase 3 tile, `§ KPI tiles`); the Top token users list filtered by the date range (SF-44).

## Lock amendments

Appended, never edited into the sections above. Each entry names the locked wording it relaxes, the user's decision behind it, what it allows and what it leaves unchanged; the locked text stays as it is and is read with these entries.

### 2026-10-18 · The newest tutor avatar may run while the answer streams

- **Relaxes:** the companion's stillness while the student reads: `§ Direction` ("it is still while the student reads") and `§ Expressive moments` 3 ("it is still while text is on screen"), the Phase 2 pick's near-zero motion while the student reads (plan Decision Log), and G9b's quiet while reading (the brief), as `§ Companion` principle 1 applied them.
- **The decision:** the user, 2026-10-18: "Kiwi 一直跑到答完再飞一下，可以放宽读字时不动的规则。" (Kiwi keeps running until the answer is complete, then flies once; the rule that it stays still while text is read may be relaxed). It settles the open detail of the same day's first decision: "kiwi的图标不应该从变成跑步的样子然后变成半身图然后又变回跳一跳飞起来，这样显得很不连贯，最好应该一直是小小的，开始生成的时候才跑动，生成完了跳一下或者飞一下，不要有割裂感" (the avatar stays small and full-body, runs when generation starts and hops or flies once when the answer is done, with no sense of a cut). Plan Decision Log: the rows "The tutor avatar keeps one small, full-body framing" and "Kiwi keeps running until the answer is complete, then flies once".
- **Allows:** the newest tutor message's avatar (32 px, the one mover) runs its `full-walk` loop continuously from the send until the turn ends, through the wait, every tool or agent step and the streaming answer text, then hops once on completion and stands (`§ Companion`).
- **Held by:** `.design-foundations/copy/check-copy.mjs` L7 (DESIGN.md) and L8 (JOURNEY.md), which compare both files with the amendment's base commit, line for line, against `lock-amendments.json`; L1 and L4 read that base, so the locked history they hold is unchanged.
- **Does not change:** every other motion rule of `§ Motion` and `§ Companion`: one moving thing per view, and nothing else in the panel animates while an answer streams; streamed text appears unanimated and reading surfaces never move; older avatars hold their still, and compare mode animates one column only; the deployment flag, `prefers-reduced-motion` and the Display menu's Kiwi animation switch, in that precedence, still reduce the avatar to its standing still; a hidden tab or an offscreen avatar pauses it; once the turn ends (completed, stopped, failed or offline) the avatar is still, so the student reads a finished answer beside a still companion.
